    "requirements_markdown": "# GitHub Collaboration Platform Core Requirements\n\nThis product is a simplified GitHub collaboration platform. Unless an atomic requirement explicitly allows more than one role, every quoted user-interface name is the exact English accessible name of the control; the role, scope, and observable-state rules stated by each atomic requirement are authoritative. Values described as existing accounts, organizations, teams, repositories, branches, files, commits, issues, milestones, pull requests, reviews, and permission relationships are predefined seed data. The application must provision those records before the corresponding scenario and supply each account through an isolated browser session; they are not extra features that a participant must expose through a private API. Generated names may contain a unique suffix, but their role, ownership, visibility, and relationships are exactly those stated in the atomic requirement. Every atomic requirement remains enabled even when seed configuration is incomplete. All write operations must be persisted on the server, checked against the current session and target-object permission, and completed atomically. Organization Owner, repository Admin, Read, Triage, Write, Maintain, and Admin permissions follow the operation-specific rules in the requirements below; these roles are not an automatic cumulative ladder. Real-time collaboration, Actions, Packages, Wiki, project boards, notification delivery, and external Git remote protocols are outside the scope of this product.\n\n## REQ-1 Identity and Access\n\nThis module defines the “account” and “session” required to access the system. An account is a persistent individual identity storing a username, verified email, and credential status; a session is a record created after successful account sign-in that represents only the current browser login state. Visitors enter the account-access page from “Sign up”, “Sign in”, or “Forgot password” on the home page; signed-in users enter security settings or sign out from the account menu. The account menu is the control in the upper-right corner of the page that displays the current signed-in account, organization list, and sign-out entry. No page, error message, or recovery flow may display a full password. After successful sign-in, the account menu displays the current user, and subsequent organization, repository, issue, and pull-request pages use this session to determine visibility and operation permissions; sign-out, password changes, and recovery must immediately change the session state visible to subsequent requests.\n\n**Type:** FOLDER\n**Dependencies:** None\n\n### REQ-1-1 Account Registration and Recovery\n\nThe account-access page is the shared page used by unauthenticated users to register, sign in, and recover a password, with the frontend showing the appropriate form and submit button for each operation. The local product does not implement an email-verification page, email delivery, or an external verification-code service — successful registration directly stores a sign-in-capable account and marks its email as verified; successful sign-in only stores a new session and does not create a duplicate account; after submitting an email for password recovery, the page directly displays the fixed verification code `123456` and the password-reset form, and only the correct code together with a compliant new password updates the password of the same account. Registration, sign-in, and recovery pages must use visible labels to identify fields and submit actions; after failure, non-sensitive input is retained, while password and password-confirmation fields must not redisplay submitted values.\n\n**Type:** FOLDER\n**Dependencies:** None\n\n#### REQ-1-1-1 Register a New GitHub Account\n\nThe registration page is the form opened by the unique link named “Create an account” from the sign-in page. The form contains exactly one textbox labeled “Username”, one textbox labeled “Email”, one password input labeled “Password”, one password input labeled “Confirm password”, one initially unchecked checkbox named “Agree to the terms”, and one enabled button named “Create account”. The username and email identify the account, while passwords must not be echoed on the page. This product directly marks the email of a successfully registered account as verified and does not provide a subsequent email-verification page. The visitor enters a unique username, email, password, and matching confirmation, checks “Agree to the terms”, and activates “Create account”. The username must consist of 1 to 39 lowercase ASCII letters, digits, or single hyphens (`-`) and must not begin or end with a hyphen; after trimming leading and trailing whitespace, the email must contain exactly one `@`, be no longer than 254 characters, and have at least one dot and non-empty domain labels after the `@`; the password must contain 12 to 128 characters, include at least one uppercase letter, one lowercase letter, one digit, and one non-alphanumeric special character, and contain no whitespace characters. The system stores a sign-in-capable account together with its verified-email status and enters the sign-in page; if the username or email conflicts, any field is missing, the email format is invalid, the terms are not accepted, the password is noncompliant, or the confirmation does not match, the system retains the username and email input, displays an itemized message beside the corresponding field, and does not create an account. The corresponding visible field message contains “Username already exists”, “Username format is invalid”, “Email format is invalid”, “Password requirements are not satisfied”, or “Agree to terms is required”, as applicable. Visitors can use this capability on the account-access page. The system validates input rules, displays field errors, and prevents duplicate accounts; on success, it directly creates an available account. Submitting several invalid fields together must show the username, email, password, and missing-terms messages together, rather than revealing only one error per submission; the submit button remains actionable so these messages can be read. For example, a username beginning with a hyphen, email “not-an-email”, password “short”, confirmation “different”, and unchecked terms produce these field errors and preserve the attempted username. A duplicate username paired with a different unused email displays “Username already exists” and retains both attempted values. A successful registration accepts a unique username such as “pw-user-<unique suffix>”, its “@example.test” email, and password “Valid-password-123!”; the sign-in form is immediately available, accepts that email, and the resulting signed-in username remains visible after reload.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-sign-up.png)\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** None\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-1-1-1: Register a New GitHub Account - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`. The visitor clicks “Sign in” on the home page to enter the account-access page, then clicks “Create an account”. The registration page displays visibly labeled Username, Email, Password, Confirm password, a terms-agreement checkbox, and the “Create account” button; the current username and email are both unused. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **WHEN:** The visitor enters a compliant 1–39-character username, a compliant email, a compliant 12–128-character password, and an identical confirmation password, checks the terms-agreement checkbox, and clicks “Create account”. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **THEN:** The system displays registration success and redirects to the sign-in page without displaying the password; the account's email can now be used for sign-in and password recovery. If the username, email, any required field, terms, or password rules are not satisfied, the system remains on the registration page, displays the reason beside the corresponding field, retains the username and email input, and does not create an account. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **THEN:** The visitor can immediately sign in with the new account and enter the workspace; after a failed submission, reopening the registration page shows that no new account corresponding to the failed input exists. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n- REQ-1-1-1: Register a New GitHub Account - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`. The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user signs in as `alice-dev` with `Valid-password-123!` and follows the visible controls for the register a new github account workflow. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **THEN:** The page displays the required headings, controls, values, and status for the register a new github account workflow. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing or reopening the visible destination, the successful result remains persisted; a rejected action leaves the original state unchanged. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n- REQ-1-1-1: Register a New GitHub Account - Scenario 3\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`. The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user signs in as `alice-dev` with `Valid-password-123!` and follows the visible controls for the register a new github account workflow. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **THEN:** The page displays the required headings, controls, values, and status for the register a new github account workflow. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing or reopening the visible destination, the successful result remains persisted; a rejected action leaves the original state unchanged. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n\n#### REQ-1-1-2 Sign In with an Existing Account\n\nThis feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-1-1-1 (Register a New GitHub Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. The sign-in page is the form on the account-access page containing “Username or email”, “Password”, and “Sign in”. A “session” is the current-browser login record saved after successful account authentication, represented in the frontend by the upper-right account menu and access to protected pages. The visitor enters a username or email and a password and submits the form. The system looks up the account and validates its credentials and availability status; if authentication fails due to an unknown username/email, incorrect password, or unavailable account, the system displays exactly the same generic failure message “Invalid credentials”, does not disclose whether the account exists or which credential was wrong, and does not create a session. After successful authentication, the system stores a session with a unique session identifier, account identifier, and active status, then redirects to the workspace accessible to that user; the session remains valid after refreshing or reopening the workspace, and subsequent protected organization, repository, and work-item requests must use this session to determine the current user.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-sign-in.png)\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-1-1-1\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-1-1-2: Sign In with an Existing Account - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`. The visitor enters the account-access page from the home page; a verified and available account exists, and the visitor has the account's username or verified email and current password. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **WHEN:** The visitor enters the username or verified email in “Username or email”, enters the correct password in “Password”, and clicks “Sign in”. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **THEN:** The system enters the user's workspace, the account menu displays the username, and the user can access organizations, repositories, and work items they are authorized to view. An unknown account, incorrect password, or unavailable account all produce exactly the same generic failure message, remain on the sign-in page, and do not display the account menu. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **THEN:** After successful sign-in, refreshing the workspace or directly reopening the previously accessible protected page still shows the signed-in user; after failed sign-in, refreshing or opening the visible protected page still requires authentication. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n- REQ-1-1-2: Sign In with an Existing Account - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`. The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user signs in as `alice-dev` with `Valid-password-123!` and follows the visible controls for the sign in with an existing account workflow. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **THEN:** The page displays the required headings, controls, values, and status for the sign in with an existing account workflow. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing or reopening the visible destination, the successful result remains persisted; a rejected action leaves the original state unchanged. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n- REQ-1-1-2: Sign In with an Existing Account - Scenario 3\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`. The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user signs in as `alice-dev` with `Valid-password-123!` and follows the visible controls for the sign in with an existing account workflow. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n- REQ-1-1-2: Sign In with an Existing Account - Scenario 4\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`. The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user signs in as `alice-dev` with `Valid-password-123!` and follows the visible controls for the sign in with an existing account workflow. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **THEN:** The page displays the required headings, controls, values, and status for the sign in with an existing account workflow. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing or reopening the visible destination, the successful result remains persisted; a rejected action leaves the original state unchanged. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n\n#### REQ-1-1-3 Recover Account Access Through a Verified Email\n\nThe password-recovery page is opened by the link “Forgot password” on the sign-in page; the fixed code is displayed as a distinct visible text value exactly “123456”, not only embedded inside a longer instruction. “Email”, “Verification code”, “New password”, and “Confirm password” are associated field labels; “Send reset link” and “Reset password” are buttons. The recovery scenario uses a newly registered account, an unknown email, a compliant replacement such as “Replacement-password-456!”, and invalid code “000000”; failure leaves the registered account able to sign in with its old email/password. In this flow, the local system does not send email, generate a copyable reset link, or call an external verification-code service. After the visitor enters an address in the Email field and clicks “Send reset link”, the page directly switches to the next step of the same recovery flow, displays the fixed verification-code text “123456” for local demonstration only, and shows visibly labeled Verification code, New password, Confirm password fields and a “Reset password” button; the page must not require access to email. To prevent disclosure through the page of whether an account exists, both registered and unknown email addresses enter the same next step and display the same fixed verification code. The system atomically updates the credentials of that account only when the recovery context is associated with a registered email, the Verification code is character-for-character equal to “123456”, the new password complies with the password rules in REQ-1-1-1, and Confirm password matches it. If the verification code is wrong, the email is unknown, the password is noncompliant, or the confirmation does not match, the page explains the reason beside the corresponding field and does not modify any account; an incorrect verification code displays “Verification code is invalid”. After successful submission, the page directly displays “Password updated” and does not produce any email or link. This local verification code has no email-delivery, expiration, or one-time-link semantics. Password fields must not echo entered values.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-password-reset.png) ![image](reference/github-password-reset-1.png)\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-1-1-1\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-1-1-3: Recover Account Access Through a Verified Email - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`. The visitor opens “Forgot password” from the account-access page; one registered account and its email exist, together with another email that corresponds to no account for comparison. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **WHEN:** The visitor submits the registered email and the unknown email separately; in each directly opened next step, the visitor enters the fixed verification code “123456”, a new password compliant with REQ-1-1-1, and the same confirmation password, then submits. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **THEN:** Both requests directly display the fixed verification code “123456” and the password-reset form, do not indicate whether the email exists, and do not require access to email or a link. Only the correct-code submission for the registered email updates that account's password and displays a success result; an unknown email, incorrect verification code, noncompliant password, or mismatched confirmation displays the reason and does not modify any account. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **THEN:** After success, sign-in with the old password fails and sign-in with the new password succeeds; after failure, the old password still works and the candidate new password does not. Refreshing the reset page must not generate an email, link, or second account. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n- REQ-1-1-3: Recover Account Access Through a Verified Email - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`. The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user signs in as `alice-dev` with `Valid-password-123!` and follows the visible controls for the recover account access through a verified email workflow. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **THEN:** The page displays the required headings, controls, values, and status for the recover account access through a verified email workflow. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing or reopening the visible destination, the successful result remains persisted; a rejected action leaves the original state unchanged. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n- REQ-1-1-3: Recover Account Access Through a Verified Email - Scenario 3\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`. The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user signs in as `alice-dev` with `Valid-password-123!` and follows the visible controls for the recover account access through a verified email workflow. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **THEN:** The page displays the required headings, controls, values, and status for the recover account access through a verified email workflow. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing or reopening the visible destination, the successful result remains persisted; a rejected action leaves the original state unchanged. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n\n### REQ-1-2 Sign Out and End the Current Web Session\n\nThis feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. The page contains exactly one button named “Account menu”; its menu contains exactly one link named “Sign out”. Activating it displays a dialog named “Sign out” with buttons named “Confirm sign out” and “Cancel”. The dialog explains that sign-out affects only the current browser session. Only “Confirm sign out” invalidates the session, while “Cancel” or closing the dialog retains the current session and page. After confirming, refresh, browser back navigation, or directly reopening a previously accessible protected account, repository, or organization page restores an unauthenticated state and displays the “Sign in” link.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-account-menu-sign-out.png)\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-1-1-2\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-1-2: Sign Out and End the Current Web Session - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`. A signed-in user has entered an organization, repository, or account-settings page they are authorized to access from the account menu and has recorded that protected page heading. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user opens the account menu and clicks “Sign out”; if a confirmation dialog appears, the user confirms instead of canceling or closing it. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **THEN:** The system returns to an unauthenticated access state, displays the “Sign in” entry, and no longer displays that user's account menu; if the user cancels the confirmation dialog, the session remains valid and the original page remains accessible. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **THEN:** When the user refreshes, navigates back in the browser, or directly returns to the recorded protected page, the system requires re-authentication and cannot restore the signed-out session. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n- REQ-1-2: Sign Out and End the Current Web Session - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`. The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user signs in as `alice-dev` with `Valid-password-123!` and follows the visible controls for the sign out and end the current web session workflow. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **THEN:** The page displays the required headings, controls, values, and status for the sign out and end the current web session workflow. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing or reopening the visible destination, the successful result remains persisted; a rejected action leaves the original state unchanged. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n\n### REQ-1-3 Change Account Password\n\nThis feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. The security form has uniquely labeled password inputs “Current password”, “New password”, and “Confirm password”, and a button “Update password”; submitting an empty current-password field must display the visible application message “Current password is required”. A failure with an incorrect current password and confirmation “does-not-match” must display the corresponding current-password or confirmation error and leave the old credentials usable. Each password-change scenario starts with its own verified account in the stated credential state and a different compliant candidate password. “Password and authentication” is the security page in account Settings used to change the current account credentials; the change affects only the current account's password record and does not alter any other accounts or resources outside the session. The user must provide the current password, a new password compliant with the REQ-1 password rules, and a character-for-character identical confirmation password; all three fields are required. The system validates the change, prevents unauthorized updates, and applies the new password to subsequent sign-in attempts. If the current password is incorrect, the new password is noncompliant, the confirmation does not match, or any field is missing, the system displays the reason in the corresponding field and does not modify the credentials; the applicable visible message is “Current password is incorrect”, “Password confirmation does not match”, or “Current password is required”. The old password remains valid and the new password must not work for sign-in. After a successful update, the page displays “Password updated”, the new password is immediately used for subsequent password-based sign-ins, and the old password must no longer work for new sign-ins.\n\nSeed password-change values are `New-password-456!` for the successful update and\n`Required-password-789!` for the missing-current-password scenario.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-password-settings.png)\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-1-1-2\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-1-3: Change Account Password - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`. A signed-in user enters Settings from the account menu and then opens Password and authentication; the user knows the current password and has prepared a new password different from the current one and compliant with REQ-1-1-1. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user enters the current password, new password, and an identical confirmation in the Current password, New password, and Confirm password fields, then clicks “Update password”. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **THEN:** The system displays a successful password-updated result. If the current password is incorrect, the new password is noncompliant, the confirmation does not match, or any field is missing, the system displays the reason beside the corresponding field and does not change the password. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **THEN:** After signing out, the user can sign in with the new password and cannot sign in with the old password; after any failed submission, the old password still works and the candidate new password does not. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n- REQ-1-3: Change Account Password - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`. The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user signs in as `alice-dev` with `Valid-password-123!` and follows the visible controls for the change account password workflow. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **THEN:** The page displays the required headings, controls, values, and status for the change account password workflow. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing or reopening the visible destination, the successful result remains persisted; a rejected action leaves the original state unchanged. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n- REQ-1-3: Change Account Password - Scenario 3\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`. The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user signs in as `alice-dev` with `Valid-password-123!` and follows the visible controls for the change account password workflow. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **THEN:** The page displays the required headings, controls, values, and status for the change account password workflow. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing or reopening the visible destination, the successful result remains persisted; a rejected action leaves the original state unchanged. Seed values: account `alice-dev`, email `alice.dev@example.test`, password `Valid-password-123!`.\n\n## REQ-2 Organization and Governance\n\nSigned-in users enter the organization list through “Your organizations” in the account menu and can create or open an organization; visitors can only view public repositories through public organization pages. The organization overview provides Repositories, People, and Teams entries. An organization Owner directly adds or removes members on People, creates teams and maintains hierarchy on Teams, and grants roles to members or teams in Manage access under repository Settings. Organization membership only determines whether a user can see the organization and can be a candidate for team/repository authorization; it does not automatically grant access to any private repository. Access to a private repository comes only from organization Owner status, a direct role grant on that repository, or direct membership in a team authorized for that repository. All changes must be reflected simultaneously in organization details, people/team lists, and the actual access results of affected repositories.\n\n**Type:** FOLDER\n**Dependencies:** REQ-1-1-2\n\n### REQ-2-1 Organization Identity and Discovery\n\nThe organization overview page uses the organization name as a heading; its “Repositories”, “People”, and “Teams” navigation entries have link roles even when visually styled as tabs. The Repositories tab lists repositories owned by the organization, the People tab lists members and their Member/Owner roles, and the Teams tab lists teams. This module supports visitors discovering an organization's public repositories and signed-in accounts creating organizations from the “Your organizations” page. The unique organization name uses the same globally unique 1–39-character lowercase ASCII format as the REQ-1 username; the display name must contain 1–100 non-empty characters after trimming leading and trailing whitespace. After successful creation, the system stores the organization object and the creating account's Owner membership relationship, and the user enters the new organization overview page; subsequent team, member, and organization-repository authorization is scoped by this organization identifier. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n**Type:** FOLDER\n**Dependencies:** REQ-1-1-2\n\n#### REQ-2-1-1 Browse Organization Repositories\n\nThe organization page presents “Repositories” as a navigation link, visually usable as a tab, and a textbox labeled “Find a repository”. Filtering updates the visible results as the user types, without a separate submit action. Each result has a link named exactly with the repository name, and opens a heading displaying “organization name/repository name”. Returning with browser Back keeps the public result available; filtering by the exact private repository name never exposes its link to a visitor. The seed organization has a public repository and a distinct private repository inaccessible to visitors. From the organization page, the user clicks the “Repositories” link, can enter a repository name in the filter box, select a public/private filter, and click a repository name in the list to open its overview. The system displays only the names, descriptions, visibility, and update times of repositories visible to the current user; clicking a result opens that repository's overview. Visitors can view public repositories on a public organization overview page; signed-in accounts can see only repositories for which they have read permission on the Repositories tab of the organization overview. The list reads repository name, description, visibility, and update time and provides no write operations; private repositories must first be validated under the already defined repository-access rules.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-organization-repositories.png)\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** None\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-2-1-1: Browse Organization Repositories - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data includes the existing organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`. A visitor has opened the overview page of a public organization, or a signed-in organization member has opened that organization's overview page; the organization contains both a public repository and a private repository that the visitor is not authorized to view. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user clicks the “Repositories” tab, enters the full name of the public repository in the filter box, selects the “Public” filter, and clicks the repository name. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **THEN:** The list shows only the name, description, visibility, and update time of repositories visible to the current user; the visitor cannot see the private repository name, and clicking the public repository opens the repository overview page titled “organization name/repository name”. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **THEN:** After returning to the organization page or refreshing it, the filtered results still do not reveal unauthorized repositories; if the visitor opens the private repository from the visible repository list, the system requires sign-in or displays that access is not permitted. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n- REQ-2-1-1: Browse Organization Repositories - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`. The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user signs in as `alice-dev` with `Valid-password-123!` and follows the visible controls for the browse organization repositories workflow. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **THEN:** The page displays the required headings, controls, values, and status for the browse organization repositories workflow. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing or reopening the visible destination, the successful result remains persisted; a rejected action leaves the original state unchanged. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n\n#### REQ-2-1-2 Create an Organization After Authentication\n\nThe account-menu link “Your organizations” opens a page with the link “New organization”. Its form has fields labeled “Organization name” and “Display name” and a button “Create organization”. The new overview uses a heading containing the organization identifier, which remains after reload. Submitting an existing identifier must show “Organization name already exists” even when the display name is also missing; it must not navigate to a heading for the existing organization. The malformed identifier “-invalid-organization” and a whitespace-only display name are rejected with the applicable field error. Seed data supplies a verified creator and an existing organization identifier for the duplicate case. A signed-in user enters a unique organization name and display information on the organization-creation page and confirms. The organization name must comply with the 1–39-character lowercase ASCII REQ-1 username format and be globally unique; the display name must contain 1–100 non-empty characters after trimming leading and trailing whitespace. The system validates these rules, creates the organization identifier, display name, and creation time, and saves the current user as both initial owner and member; if the name or display name is noncompliant, the name conflicts, or persistence fails, the system retains non-sensitive input, displays the reason in the corresponding field, and does not create the organization. The applicable visible message is “Organization name already exists”, “Organization name format is invalid”, or “Display name is required”. On success, the user is redirected to the new organization overview page, the organization appears in the current user's accessible-organization list, and subsequent team, member, and repository authorization is scoped by that organization identifier. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-1-1-2\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-2-1-2: Create an Organization After Authentication - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`. A signed-in user enters the “Your organizations” page from the account menu and clicks “New organization”; the candidate organization identifier is `mobile-guild` and does not exist in the system, and the creation form displays organization-identifier and display-name fields. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user enters organization identifier `mobile-guild`, a display name `Mobile Guild`, and clicks “Create organization”. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **THEN:** The system enters the new organization overview page, whose title displays the organization identifier; the current user is shown as Owner and organization member, and the organization appears in the “Your organizations” list. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing the overview page and reopening the organization list, the organization still exists; if the organization identifier is duplicated, malformed, or the display name is empty, the corresponding field displays the reason and no organization is created. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n- REQ-2-1-2: Create an Organization After Authentication - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **THEN:** The page displays the required headings, controls, values, and status for the create an organization after authentication workflow. The seeded data is organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user signs in as `alice-dev` with `Valid-password-123!` and follows the visible controls for the create an organization after authentication workflow. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **THEN:** The page displays the required headings, controls, values, and status for the create an organization after authentication workflow. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing or reopening the visible destination, the successful result remains persisted; a rejected action leaves the original state unchanged. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n\n### REQ-2-2 Team and Member Management\n\nOn the current organization's member- and team-management pages, users create teams, maintain team hierarchy and membership relationships, and directly add or remove organization members. A team name contains 1–50 lowercase ASCII letters, digits, or hyphens, may not begin or end with a hyphen, and must be unique within the same organization; a team must belong to an organization, and a parent team must belong to the same organization and may not create a direct or indirect cycle. Organization membership and team membership are independent persisted relationships; team hierarchy is used only for display and management and does not implicitly add members of a parent or child team to the other team. On the People page, an Owner enters the username or verified email of an existing account and confirms, after which the system immediately saves that account's Member or Owner membership relationship; there is no invitation, Pending, or acceptance step. When removing a member, the system atomically deletes that organization-membership relationship, all of that account's team memberships in the organization, and all direct grants to that account on repositories of the organization; team grants themselves are not deleted. If removal would leave the organization with no Owner, the system rejects the operation and keeps all relationships unchanged; the person's account, personal repositories, and relationships with other organizations must not be deleted. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-2-1-2 (Create an Organization After Authentication); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n**Type:** FOLDER\n**Dependencies:** REQ-2-1-2\n\n#### REQ-2-2-1 Create an Organization Team\n\nThe organization overview has a “Teams” link; that page has a “New team” link opening the field “Team name” and button “Create team”. A unique compliant name can be submitted without a description or parent. The resulting team heading contains its name and remains visible after reload. Seed data supplies an organization and an account with Owner permission. A team page is the detail page for a grouping of accounts within an organization, titled “organization name/team name”; the Members tab displays the team's direct members, and Settings is used to change the parent team. Only an organization Owner can click “New team” from the Teams tab on the organization overview. The system stores the team identifier, owning organization, name, optional description, optional parent team, creator, and timestamp; if the team name is missing, malformed, duplicated, the parent team does not belong to the current organization, or creation fails, no team is generated. A malformed team name displays “Team name format is invalid”. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-2-1-2 (Create an Organization After Authentication); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-2-1-2\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-2-2-1: Create an Organization Team - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`. An organization Owner is signed in and has opened “Teams” from the organization overview page; the organization may already contain a parent team, and the target team name `mobile-team` is not yet used in that organization. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **WHEN:** The Owner clicks “New team”, enters team name `mobile-team`, which is 1 to 50 characters long containing only lowercase ASCII letters, digits, or hyphens and not beginning or ending with a hyphen, optionally enters a description and a parent team from the same organization, then clicks “Create team”. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **THEN:** The system redirects to the new team page, whose title displays “organization name/team name”; the team tree displays its organization and selected parent team. If the name is empty, malformed, duplicated, or the parent team does not belong to the organization, an error is displayed and no team is created. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing the team page or reopening the “Teams” list, the new team and its parent-child relationship remain consistent. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n- REQ-2-2-1: Create an Organization Team - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`. The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user signs in as `alice-dev` with `Valid-password-123!` and follows the visible controls for the create an organization team workflow. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **THEN:** The page displays the required headings, controls, values, and status for the create an organization team workflow. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing or reopening the visible destination, the successful result remains persisted; a rejected action leaves the original state unchanged. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n\n#### REQ-2-2-2 Manage Organization Team Members and Hierarchy\n\nOnly an organization Owner may maintain members or hierarchy on an organization team page. The team maintainer in these scenarios is an organization Owner. The team page has links “Members” and “Settings”. On Members, the button “Add member” opens a textbox labeled “Username” and the submit button “Add member”; only the current step's Add member button is exposed as an actionable match. After adding, the member's complete username is visible once in the member list, alongside a button named “Remove <username>”. Activating this removal button immediately removes that member without a second confirmation step, and reload keeps the username absent. Settings contains a native select with combobox role labeled “Parent team”, whose option labels are team names, and a button “Save”. The original parent remains selected after a rejected change and reload. The cycle scenario supplies a team with an existing parent and a descendant selectable by name; submitting that descendant shows the cycle error rather than saving it. The seed record's original-parent value identifies the stored selection, which may differ from its display label. The membership scenario supplies a current organization member not yet in this team. On the team page, the Owner clicks “Members” to add or remove current organization members, or selects a new parent team in “Settings” and saves. The system stores the team-membership relationship or team hierarchy and immediately reflects it in the organization team tree and member list; team hierarchy creates no implicit membership relationship or repository permission, and cyclic hierarchy is prohibited. A rejected cycle displays “Cyclic team hierarchy is not allowed”. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-2-2-1 (Create an Organization Team); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-2-2-1\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-2-2-2: Manage Organization Team Members and Hierarchy - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`. A signed-in team maintainer opens a team page within an organization; there is an organization member who has not yet joined the team and a candidate parent team in the same organization that would not create a cycle. The member is `bob-reviewer` and the team is `frontend-team`. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **WHEN:** On the “Members” page, the maintainer uses “Add member” to precisely search for and add that organization member, then selects the candidate parent team in “Settings” and saves; the maintainer subsequently removes the just-added member from the member action menu. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **THEN:** After addition, the member list displays the account; after saving, the team tree displays the new parent-child relationship; after removal, the account no longer appears in the member list. Non-organization members cannot be added, and any selection that would make the team its own parent or the parent of one of its ancestors is rejected. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing the member page and organization team tree, the page displays only successfully saved parent-team and membership relationships. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n- REQ-2-2-2: Manage Organization Team Members and Hierarchy - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`. A team maintainer opens an organization team that already has a parent team. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **WHEN:** The maintainer selects one of that team's descendant teams as the new parent team and saves. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **THEN:** The system rejects the cyclic relationship, keeps the original parent team unchanged, and continues to display the original hierarchy after reload. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n\n#### REQ-2-2-3 Directly Add a User as an Organization Member\n\nThe organization overview opens “People” through a link. Its button “Add member” opens the labeled field “Username or email”, a combobox “Role” defaulting to Member, and a submission button “Add member”. Opening Role exposes clickable options “Member” and “Owner”. If the opening button remains present, the form submission button follows it in page order. The successful People list displays the full target username and Member role, with no Pending or Awaiting state; reload preserves the row. On duplicate or unknown-account failure, keep the form open so the username can be corrected and resubmitted; the existing member's complete username appears only once in the People list. A separately signed-in new member sees the organization identifier as complete visible text in Your organizations, while an ungranted private repository shows “Access denied”. The scenarios provide an Owner, a registered nonmember, a different existing member, an unknown username, and a private organization repository with no direct or team grant to the new member. On the “People” page, an organization Owner clicks “Add member”, enters the username or verified email of an existing account, selects “Member” or “Owner”, and clicks “Add member”. The system atomically stores the “organization-account-role” membership relationship and immediately displays the organization in both the People list and the target account's “Your organizations” list; the target account can see the organization on its next sign-in, after refreshing the workspace, or after reopening the organization overview. A Member gains only organization visibility and eligibility to become a candidate for team/repository authorization; an Owner additionally gains Admin permission over the organization and its repositories. For an existing member, unknown account, unsupported role, or persistence failure, the system displays the reason and does not change the membership relationship; an existing member displays “Account is already a member”, and the unknown username `unknown-reviewer` displays “Account not found”. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-2-1-2 (Create an Organization After Authentication); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-2-1-2\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-2-2-3: Directly Add a User as an Organization Member - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`. An organization Owner is signed in and has opened the organization's “People” page; the system contains a registered account that is not yet a member of the organization and can subsequently sign in independently. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **WHEN:** The Owner clicks “Add member”, enters that account's username or verified email, selects “Member”, and clicks “Add member”. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **THEN:** The “People” page immediately displays the account with the Member role and no Pending state; after that account signs in, both “Your organizations” in the account menu and the organization overview display the organization, but the account has not gained access to any private repository that was not separately authorized. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **THEN:** After the Owner refreshes the “People” page and the target account signs in and refreshes the “Your organizations” page, both read the same membership relationship; an existing member, unknown account, unsupported role, or persistence failure does not create a duplicate membership relationship. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n- REQ-2-2-3: Directly Add a User as an Organization Member - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`. The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user signs in as `alice-dev` with `Valid-password-123!` and follows the visible controls for the directly add a user as an organization member workflow. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **THEN:** The page displays the required headings, controls, values, and status for the directly add a user as an organization member workflow. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing or reopening the visible destination, the successful result remains persisted; a rejected action leaves the original state unchanged. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n\n#### REQ-2-2-4 Remove a Member from an Organization\n\nOpen the organization's “People” link to read member usernames. Each removable member has a button named “Member menu <username>”; it opens the menuitem “Remove from organization”, followed by the confirmation button “Remove”. Successful removal makes the complete username absent from People immediately and after reload. A non-Owner viewing the same member has no member-menu button and no Remove from organization menuitem, rather than merely a disabled control. Each independent scenario starts with that member present; the non-Owner is an ordinary organization member. On the “People” page, an organization Owner locates a member, clicks “Remove from organization” in that member's action menu, and clicks “Remove” in the confirmation dialog. The system atomically deletes that member's organization-membership relationship, all of the account's memberships in teams of that organization, and all direct grants to that account on repositories of the organization; if the account still obtains repository permission through another organization Owner status or an undeleted team grant, effective permission is recalculated according to the valid permission rules, otherwise access is denied. The system must not delete the personal account, personal repositories, relationships with other organizations, teams themselves, or team grants; the last Owner must not be removed. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-2-1-2 (Create an Organization After Authentication); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-2-1-2\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-2-2-4: Remove a Member from an Organization - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`. An organization Owner is signed in and has opened the organization's “People” page; the list contains a member who also has an accessible personal account and personal repository. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **WHEN:** The Owner locates the row by the member's username, clicks “Remove from organization” in the action menu, and clicks “Remove” in the confirmation dialog. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **THEN:** The system removes the account from the organization-member list and revokes access obtained solely through that organization membership; direct access by the account to private organization resources is denied. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing the “People” page, the account is still absent from the member list; after signing in with that account, the personal account and personal repository still exist and remain accessible. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n- REQ-2-2-4: Remove a Member from an Organization - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`. The attempted descendant is `frontend-child` and the original parent is `platform-team`. A signed-in user who is not an organization Owner opens the organization's People page and can see a member. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user opens the member action menu and attempts to remove that member. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **THEN:** The removal operation is unavailable to the non-Owner or is rejected, and after reload the member still belongs to the organization. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n\n### REQ-2-3 Grant Repository Access to People and Teams\n\nFrom the repository's “Settings” link, open the “Manage access” link and activate “Add people or teams”. The picker presents a textbox “Search”, a matching selectable team option whose name contains the team name, a combobox “Role” with clickable role options including “Write”, and an “Add” button. While the picker is active, hide the opening Add people or teams button from the active view so the submit action is unambiguous. After saving, the authorization list visibly shows the exact team name and Write role and retains the team after reload. Existing grants appear as rows whose accessible names include the subject name; each row contains a native select labeled “Role” and a “Save” button. Selecting the option labeled “Read” and saving replaces Write; after reload exactly one row for the team remains and contains Read. The creation scenario supplies a repository Admin and an organization team not yet granted access; the replacement scenario supplies a separate repository/team with an existing Write grant. An organization Owner or repository Admin may grant a repository role of Read, Triage, Write, Maintain, or Admin to a current organization member or team. In the access-subject picker, matching member or team options update as the administrator types; pressing Enter or activating a separate search button is not required before selecting an option. A private repository is accessible only to an organization Owner, a member with a direct repository role, or a direct member of a team that has a repository role; an ordinary organization Member has no default private-repository access. For a given subject and repository, only one direct role grant is stored — saving the same role again does not create a second record, and changing the role replaces the original role. An account's effective repository role is the highest among Admin from Owner status and all still-valid direct grants and team grants; team hierarchy does not propagate membership or authorization. The system stores the authorization subject, repository, role, grantor, and timestamp and authorizes operations according to the ROOT role matrix; unauthorized accounts cannot see private repositories. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-2-1-2 (Create an Organization After Authentication) and REQ-2-2-1 (Create an Organization Team); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-2-1-2, REQ-2-2-1\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-2-3: Grant Repository Access to People and Teams - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`. An organization Owner or repository Admin is signed in and has opened the “Manage access” page of a private organization repository; the organization contains one member and one team, neither of which currently has a direct grant on that repository. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **WHEN:** The administrator selects the member or team, selects the “Write” role, and clicks “Add” or “Save”. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **THEN:** The authorization list displays exactly one “Write” role record for that subject; when the member or a member of the team opens the repository, they can view repository content and perform operations allowed by the Write role, while accounts that were not granted permission do not gain that access. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing the “Manage access” page, the grant still exists; saving the same role again for the same subject does not generate a second authorization record. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n- REQ-2-3: Grant Repository Access to People and Teams - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`. An administrator opens a repository-access management page where a grant has already been saved for a team. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **WHEN:** The administrator changes that team's role to another level and saves the update. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n  - **THEN:** The system retains only one team-authorization record, replaces the old role with the new role, and displays the updated grant after reload. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.\n\n## REQ-3 Repository Asset Management\n\nA repository is a container for code, branches, commits, files, and collaborative work items. The repository overview page is titled “owner/repository name”, displays the owner, name, Public/Private marker, description, and default branch, and provides Code, Issues, Pull requests, and Settings entries; the “owner” is an individual account or organization, and a repository belonging to an organization is an organization repository. Users open a repository overview from global search results, a personal/organization Repositories list, or a direct link; signed-in users with creation permission create a personal or organization repository from “New repository”. Public repositories allow visitor Read access; private personal repositories allow access only to their owner and explicitly authorized subjects; private organization repositories allow access only to organization Owners, members with direct repository roles, or direct members of teams with repository roles, and ordinary organization Members do not automatically gain access merely through membership. After visibility or access grants change, search, lists, direct links, and repository pages must all use the same access rule.\n\n**Type:** FOLDER\n**Dependencies:** None\n\n### REQ-3-1 Search for and Locate Repositories\n\nThe top global search control has searchbox role and accessible name “Search”; entering a repository name and pressing Enter displays repository results directly, without requiring an additional type-filter click. Each repository result has a link whose accessible name is exactly its repository name, while the result also displays its owner/name metadata. Clicking it opens a heading containing the repository name; reloading preserves that overview. A query with no matching repository displays “No results”, including when the visitor returns home and repeats the same query. Seed data provides a named public repository and a private repository inaccessible to visitors; searching the private name exposes no result link for it. Visitors or signed-in users can search repositories they are authorized to view. The system accepts a search query, applies repository-oriented filters or scope constraints, and displays matching repositories with sufficient metadata for the user to open the target repository.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-repository-search-results.png)\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** None\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-3-1: Search for and Locate Repositories - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. A “fork” is an independent repository copied from a readable source repository — the frontend displays the source link on the fork overview page, and the system stores the source-repository identifier; branches and commits in the fork may evolve independently and never write back to the source repository. A “clone page entry” is a read-only address displayed in the repository Code popover and can be copied to the clipboard; it does not mean this product implements an external Git protocol. Both new repositories and forks must enter a browsable repository overview page. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n**Type:** FOLDER\n**Dependencies:** REQ-1-1-2\n\n#### REQ-3-2-1 Create a Repository with Owner, Visibility, and Initialization Options\n\nThis feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. The signed-in workspace provides a “New repository” link. The form labels are “Owner”, “Repository name”, and “Description”; the signed-in user's personal namespace is selected by default, so it is possible to submit without changing the owner. Visibility choices are radio controls “Public” and “Private”; initialization is a checkbox “Add a README file”; submission uses the button “Create repository”. Other defaults must allow duplicate-name and empty-name validation without further input. A successful initialized private repository shows a heading containing the new name, a visible Private marker, and a README file link; the overview persists after reload. The optional description “Repository created by Playwright” is saved when entered. An existing repository in this same default personal namespace is supplied for duplicate validation, which stays on the form and does not open the existing repository heading. On the repository-creation page, a signed-in user selects a personal or organization owner, enters a repository name and description, selects public or private visibility, and chooses whether to create an initial commit with a README. The system verifies that the user may create a repository in that owner's namespace, that the name is unique, and that the options are valid; on success, it stores the repository identifier, owner, visibility, default branch, and creator. If initialization is selected, it must also atomically create the initial branch, README file, and initial commit; failure at any step must leave no partially created repository. On success, the new repository overview page is opened.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-create-repository.png)\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-1-1-2\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-3-2-1: Create a Repository with Owner, Visibility, and Initialization Options - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session.
