The fork form contains a field labeled “Repository name” and button “Create fork”; it defaults to the signed-in user's personal namespace and an allowed visibility, permitting submission after editing only the name. Seed data supplies a readable source, a user allowed to create personal repositories, and an existing fork name in that namespace for the conflict case. Success opens a heading containing the new fork name and visible text “Forked from <source repository name>” with a source link; the heading and source relationship survive reload. On the source repository page, the user clicks “Fork”, selects a target personal or organization namespace, enters an optional new name and visibility, and clicks “Create fork”. The system creates an independent fork, copies the accessible default-branch history, records the source-repository link, and then opens the new fork overview page. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-3-3 (View a Public Repository Overview) and REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. A signed-in user can fork only when they have Read or higher permission on the source repository and permission to create repositories in the target personal or organization namespace; when the source repository is private, the fork must remain Private, while a public source repository may be forked as Public or Private. The system validates permissions on both ends and stores the new fork's owner, visibility, source-repository identifier, default branch, and copied accessible history; if any check fails, no fork is created.\n\nThe fork conflict seed uses the existing name `acme-docs-fork` in the target\npersonal namespace.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-fork-repository.png)\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-3-3, REQ-1-1-2\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-3-2-2: Fork a Repository into Another Namespace - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: public repository `acme-docs`, private repository `secret-research`, owner `alice-dev`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing the fork page and target owner's repository list, the fork and source link still exist; if the target name conflicts, the user lacks permission in the target namespace, or the source repository is inaccessible, no fork is created. Seed values: public repository `acme-docs`, private repository `secret-research`, owner `alice-dev`.\n\n#### REQ-3-2-3 Copy a Repository Clone Value\n\nThe clone popover is opened by a button “Code”, separate from the repository navigation link of the same name. Protocol controls are tabs “HTTPS” and “SSH”, and the selected clone value has a button “Copy clone value”. The HTTPS clone value uses the HTTPS protocol; the SSH clone value uses the selected SSH format, including the colon and .git suffix. Both must identify the current repository. With browser clipboard permission, copying writes the selected complete value, displays “Copied”, and leaves the repository heading visible. Seed data provides a visitor-readable public repository; clipboard permission is a browser precondition. On the repository overview page, the user clicks “Code”, selects the HTTPS or SSH clone method, and then clicks the copy icon to the right of the clone value. The system writes the selected clone value to the browser clipboard and displays brief “Copied” feedback; this read-only operation does not modify the repository. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-3-3 (View a Public Repository Overview); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. Visitors or signed-in users may use this capability on a repository page. This capability requires the target public object to be available or the user to have view permission. The system verifies eligibility, displays any required warning or unavailable state, and prevents unauthorized changes. The selected repository-access value can be pasted by the user into a local tool.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-code-clone-menu.png)\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-3-3\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-3-2-3: Copy a Repository Clone Value - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: public repository `acme-docs`, private repository `secret-research`, owner `alice-dev`.\n- REQ-3-2-3: Copy a Repository Clone Value - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is public repository `acme-docs`, private repository `secret-research`, owner `alice-dev`. The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: public repository `acme-docs`, private repository `secret-research`, owner `alice-dev`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user signs in as `alice-dev` with `Valid-password-123!` and follows the visible controls for the copy a repository clone value workflow. Seed values: public repository `acme-docs`, private repository `secret-research`, owner `alice-dev`.\n  - **THEN:** The page displays the required headings, controls, values, and status for the copy a repository clone value workflow. Seed values: public repository `acme-docs`, private repository `secret-research`, owner `alice-dev`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing or reopening the visible destination, the successful result remains persisted; a rejected action leaves the original state unchanged. Seed values: public repository `acme-docs`, private repository `secret-research`, owner `alice-dev`.\n\n### REQ-3-3 View a Public Repository Overview\n\nThe repository overview identifies the repository in a heading containing “owner/repository name”, displays a visible “Public” marker for a public repository, and provides a navigation link “Code”. This link is distinct from the clone-menu Code button. A seeded public repository with its matching name and stable address is readable without sign-in and retains the same heading after reload. Visitors or authorized users can open a repository overview to confirm repository identity, visibility, description, primary metadata, and available content. The overview provides the primary entry points for browsing files, viewing repository context, and beginning collaboration.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-repository-overview.png)\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** None\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-3-3: View a Public Repository Overview - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is public repository `acme-docs`, private repository `secret-research`, owner `alice-dev`. A visitor has a search result, personal/organization repository-list item, or direct address for a public repository; the repository has a description, default branch, and at least one file. Seed values: public repository `acme-docs`, private repository `secret-research`, owner `alice-dev`.\n  - **WHEN:** The visitor clicks the repository name or directly opens the repository address. Seed values: public repository `acme-docs`, private repository `secret-research`, owner `alice-dev`.\n  - **THEN:** The overview page displays “owner/repository name”, the Public marker, description, default branch, and file list, and provides entries such as “Code”, “Issues”, and “Pull requests”; the visitor can click a file name to enter the file-content page. Seed values: public repository `acme-docs`, private repository `secret-research`, owner `alice-dev`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing or reopening through the same address, the same repository state is still displayed; if the address is replaced with that of a private repository for which the visitor has no authorization, its content is not shown to the visitor. Seed values: public repository `acme-docs`, private repository `secret-research`, owner `alice-dev`.\n\n### REQ-3-4 Change Repository Visibility with Permission Checks\n\nThis feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-3-3 (View a Public Repository Overview) and REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. Open the repository's “Settings” link and then its “General” link to reach Danger Zone. The visibility action is a button “Change visibility”; the confirmation flow provides a “Public” radio and a button “Confirm visibility”. Selecting Public and activating Confirm visibility completes the change without requiring the repository name to be retyped or any additional mandatory field. A non-Admin collaborator must not see the Change visibility button, including when a Settings link is available. Seed data supplies an Admin, a distinct non-Admin collaborator, and a currently private repository containing public-ready content. After successful confirmation, a visible Public marker appears, and an unauthenticated visitor reopening the same address sees its repository heading. A repository administrator changes repository visibility after reviewing the impact on access permissions. The system blocks unauthorized changes, applies the selected visibility, and enforces the updated access rules when users subsequently open the repository.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-repository-visibility-settings.png)\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-3-3, REQ-1-1-2\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-3-4: Change Repository Visibility with Permission Checks - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is public repository `acme-docs`, private repository `secret-research`, owner `alice-dev`. A repository Admin is signed in and has opened the overview page of a repository that is currently Private; there is also an unauthenticated visitor, and the repository already contains files and a description suitable for public display. Seed values: public repository `acme-docs`, private repository `secret-research`, owner `alice-dev`.\n  - **WHEN:** The Admin enters “Settings” → “General” → “Danger Zone”, selects changing visibility to Public, clicks “Change visibility”, enters the full repository name as required by the page in the confirmation dialog, and confirms. Seed values: public repository `acme-docs`, private repository `secret-research`, owner `alice-dev`.\n  - **THEN:** The system displays the Public marker on the repository overview page; after the visitor refreshes, the visitor can view repository content from the direct address, and search and repository lists also display the repository according to the public-access rules. Seed values: public repository `acme-docs`, private repository `secret-research`, owner `alice-dev`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing the settings page, visibility remains Public; a non-Admin either does not see an actionable visibility-change entry or has the submission rejected, and if the confirmation text does not match, visibility remains Private. Seed values: public repository `acme-docs`, private repository `secret-research`, owner `alice-dev`.\n- REQ-3-4: Change Repository Visibility with Permission Checks - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is public repository `acme-docs`, private repository `secret-research`, owner `alice-dev`. The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: public repository `acme-docs`, private repository `secret-research`, owner `alice-dev`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user signs in as `alice-dev` with `Valid-password-123!` and follows the visible controls for the change repository visibility with permission checks workflow. Seed values: public repository `acme-docs`, private repository `secret-research`, owner `alice-dev`.\n  - **THEN:** The page displays the required headings, controls, values, and status for the change repository visibility with permission checks workflow. Seed values: public repository `acme-docs`, private repository `secret-research`, owner `alice-dev`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing or reopening the visible destination, the successful result remains persisted; a rejected action leaves the original state unchanged. Seed values: public repository `acme-docs`, private repository `secret-research`, owner `alice-dev`.\n\n## REQ-4 Code and Version Control\n\nThe Code page is the main page for browsing code in a repository — the top of the page displays the current branch selector, the main area displays files and directories at the current path on the current branch, clicking a file opens a read-only file page, and clicking Commits opens commit history. A file is content at a path on a branch; a directory is only a path hierarchy used for organization and is not an independent collaboration object. A branch is a named reference pointing to a commit, and switching branches only changes the file snapshot read by the current page. A commit is an immutable change record that stores the parent commit, author, message, time, and changed files; users with Write, Maintain, Admin, or organization Owner permission can create new commits from a file page through Edit or Add file. Every file, commit, comparison, and branch page displays the repository and current branch or revision. Editing a file must create a new commit and must not rewrite historical content. Seed data for read-only scenarios consists of public repositories and directly openable repository, file, and commit pages; visitors need not sign in to browse files, history, diffs, code search, or existing branches. Write scenarios provide separate existing contributor accounts with Write or higher permission on their target repositories and an unprotected writable branch; default-branch scenarios provide an Admin and a separate readable non-Admin account. These accounts sign in through the home-page “Sign in” link, the “Username or email” and “Password” fields, and the “Sign in” button; the signed-in username is visible before opening the target repository entry. Unless a narrower scope is explicitly given below, each named control is unique on the active page, and field and option names refer to accessible names.\n\n**Type:** FOLDER\n**Dependencies:** REQ-3-3\n\n### REQ-4-1 Browse Repository Files and Directories\n\nThis feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-3-3 (View a Public Repository Overview); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. Any user with repository-view permission can browse directories and files on the selected branch from the Code page; a directory page displays the current path, while a file page reads the stored content, most recent commit, and file name for that branch and path without creating file changes. Seed data includes a known directory on the default branch, a known text file immediately inside it, and its expected content. The directory and file entries are links whose exact accessible names are their respective directory and file names. Clicking them in order opens the saved text, with the expected content visible as a complete text value; reloading that file page retains the same branch, path, and content.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-code-file-browser.png)\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-3-3\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-4-1: Browse Repository Files and Directories - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`. The user has opened the “Code” page of an accessible repository; the current branch contains files in the root directory, a nested directory, and a text file inside that directory. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user clicks the nested-directory name and then clicks the text-file name inside it. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n  - **THEN:** The directory page displays the current branch, path breadcrumbs, and the file list for that directory; the file page displays the full path, current branch, and readable file content, and the breadcrumbs can return to the parent directory. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n  - **THEN:** After switching to another branch that does not contain the file, the file is not displayed; refreshing the current file page does not modify file content or the branch. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n\n### REQ-4-2 Commit History and Code Search\n\nThe “commit history” page displays commit records for a branch or file path in reverse chronological order; the “diff” page compares the file contents of two commits or revisions line by line, where base is the earlier or target revision and compare is the newer or to-be-compared revision; the “code search” page searches for keywords only within readable file content in the current repository. All three are read-only views that do not create commits or modify files or branches and follow repository visibility and access permissions. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-4-1 (Browse Repository Files and Directories); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n**Type:** FOLDER\n**Dependencies:** REQ-4-1\n\n#### REQ-4-2-1 View Repository Commit History\n\nThis feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-4-1 (Browse Repository Files and Directories); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. Any user with repository-view permission can read branch history on the Commits page, or from a file page read only the history that modified that file; each record references the stored commit identifier, author, time, message, parent commit, and changed files. The repository and file pages each expose one history link named “Commits” (a commit count may accompany the visible label without introducing another matching link). Seed history includes a known commit message and author, displayed in full as distinct readable text values, and a relative timestamp containing “ago”. Opening the branch history directly from the repository must show these values without first selecting a file-specific scope.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-commit-history.png)\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-4-1\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-4-2-1: View Repository Commit History - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`. The user has opened the default branch of an accessible repository; the branch contains at least two commits, and the later commit modified a known file. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user clicks the commit-count link above the file list or “Commits”, then selects the history scope for the known file. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n  - **THEN:** The history page displays commits within that scope with the newest first; each item shows the short hash, commit message, author, and time, and the file-scoped history does not display commits that did not modify that file. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n  - **THEN:** After clicking any commit to enter its detail page, the corresponding parent revision and changed files remain visible; refreshing the history page does not change commit order or content. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n\n#### REQ-4-2-2 Inspect Commit and Revision Differences\n\nThis feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-4-2-1 (View Repository Commit History); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. Any user with repository-view permission can compare two readable commits or revisions on the diff page; the page reads and displays the changed files and line-by-line additions and deletions between them without creating a review, comment, or commit. A seeded commit has an accessible commit entry, a known changed-file path `src/search.ts`, and a readable parent revision. Opening that commit entry as a visitor displays the changed-file path as an exact text value, the “Changed files” summary, and the numeric additions/deletions summary described below, without requiring previous navigation through history.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-commit-diff.png)\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-4-2-1\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-4-2-2: Inspect Commit and Revision Differences - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n  - **THEN:** The diff page displays the base and compare identifiers, the list of changed files, and added/deleted lines for each file; clicking a changed file navigates to that file's diff, and unchanged files do not appear in the list. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n  - **THEN:** Refreshing or returning to commit history does not cause the read-only comparison to modify repository branches, commits, or files; if the user lacks permission to view either revision, its diff content is not displayed. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n\n#### REQ-4-2-3 Search Code Within a Repository\n\nThe user enters a code keyword in the search box at the top of the repository page and presses Enter, selects the “Code” results page, can further filter by path or language, and clicks a matching file. The system displays matching snippets, file paths, and branch/revision context within the visible code scope of the current repository; clicking a result opens the corresponding file location. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-4-1 (Browse Repository Files and Directories); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. Visitors can search from the Code page of a public repository, while signed-in accounts can search any repository they are authorized to view; the results page only reads file content, matching snippets, and paths from currently visible branches of the current repository and cannot leak results across repositories or write code. The repository page and search results expose one searchbox named “Search”; after Enter, a unique link named “Code” selects code results. This results-type link must remain distinguishable from repository navigation so the active search page has only one Code link. Path and language filters are optional, and an unfiltered search can immediately open a result link whose exact accessible name is the matching file name. Seed data provides the known query `search flow`, a matching file `README.md` with that query visible as a complete text value, and the absent query `no-such-token`, which does not occur in searchable code. Opening the matching file shows that text; refreshing preserves the file context, text, and a link named exactly after the file. For the absent query, show “No code results” and retain the exact query in Search. Returning to the repository and repeating the same search produces the same empty state without stale matches.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-repository-code-search.png)\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-4-1\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-4-2-3: Search Code Within a Repository - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n  - **THEN:** The page displays the required headings, controls, values, and status for the search code within a repository workflow. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n\n### REQ-4-3 Branch Management\n\nThe branch selector is the control at the top of the Code page that lists and switches branches; the system stores a unique name and current pointed-to commit for each branch. Creating a branch creates a new named reference at a specified base commit; it does not copy files or rewrite base history. The default branch is the branch read by default when opening the repository overview or a repository entry without a specified branch; it is not the only existing or writable branch. A branch name is 1–255 characters and may contain only ASCII letters, digits, `-`, `_`, `.`, and `/`; it must not end with `/` or `.`, and must not contain consecutive `..` or `//`; duplicate names, invalid names, or lack of permission must prevent branch creation. Changing the default branch must not alter existing branches, commits, or source/target references of already-open PRs. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-4-1 (Browse Repository Files and Directories); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n**Type:** FOLDER\n**Dependencies:** REQ-4-1\n\n#### REQ-4-3-1 List and Switch Repository Branches\n\nThis feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-4-1 (Browse Repository Files and Directories); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. Any user with repository-view permission can use the branch selector at the top of the Code page to list and switch branches; the “current branch” is the branch jointly referenced by the selector, page entry, and file list, not an additionally persisted user setting. Matching branch options or the “No matching branch” state update as the user types in the branch textbox; pressing Enter or activating a separate search button is not required. The selector is a unique button named “Branch <current branch name>”; it opens a textbox named “Find branch” and selectable items with role option whose exact accessible names are the branch names. Seed data includes active branch `main`, target branch `feature-search` containing the file `main-only.md` absent from `main`, and an unknown search term matching no branch. Selecting the target immediately updates the button's branch name and shows a link with the exact target-only file name. Escape closes the selector; for an unmatched search it preserves the original active branch before and after reload.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-branch-selector.png)\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-4-1\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-4-3-1: List and Switch Repository Branches - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`. The user has opened the `main` branch of an accessible repository; the repository also contains a `feature-search` branch, and the two branches differ in the content of a known file. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user opens the branch selector currently showing `main`, enters `feature-search`, and clicks that branch name. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n  - **THEN:** The page, branch selector, and file list all switch to `feature-search`, and the known file displays the content from that branch; the selector can list both `main` and the target branch and marks the current branch. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n  - **THEN:** Selecting `main` again restores its file content; switching branches does not create a commit or change either branch head. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n- REQ-4-3-1: List and Switch Repository Branches - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`. A user with repository-view permission has opened the branch selector on the Code page, and the selector currently displays an existing branch. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n  - **WHEN:** The participant enters a branch query with no matching result. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n  - **THEN:** The system displays an empty branch-search state and retains the current active branch after the selector is closed or the page is reloaded. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n\n#### REQ-4-3-2 Create a Branch from an Existing Revision\n\nThe user opens the branch selector, enters a new branch name, confirms the displayed base branch or commit, and clicks “Create branch: <name>”. As the user types, a valid unused name displays the “Create branch: <name>” option without requiring Enter or a separate search action, while an invalid name immediately displays “Invalid branch”. The system creates a new branch pointing to that base commit and switches the selector to it; if the name already exists or the user lacks permission, no branch is created. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-4-3-1 (List and Switch Repository Branches) and REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. Only signed-in users with Write, Maintain, Admin, or organization Owner status may create branches on repository code and version-control pages; Read and Triage may only browse. The system validates the role, stores the new branch name, base commit, creator, and time, and rejects unauthorized requests. The selector and “Find branch” textbox use REQ-4-3-1's roles. For a valid unused name, the creation entry has role option and accessible name “Create branch: <name>”. Its displayed base defaults to the current branch head; selecting this option performs creation and switches branches without a second mandatory confirmation. Reloading the resulting page entry keeps the newly created branch selected. In particular, entering `invalid..branch` immediately shows “Invalid branch” and cannot create a reference; generated valid names such as `pw-branch-<unique suffix>` follow the same rules.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-create-branch.png)\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-4-3-1, REQ-1-1-2\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-4-3-2: Create a Branch from an Existing Revision - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`. A signed-in user with write permission has opened the repository's `main` branch; the current branch-head commit is known, and the target name `feature/api-v2` does not exist. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user opens the branch selector, enters `feature/api-v2`, confirms that the base is the current `main` branch-head commit, and clicks “Create branch: feature/api-v2”. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n  - **THEN:** The system creates the branch and switches the browsing context to it; the initial commit in the branch details matches the original `main` branch head, and the original branch history and files are not rewritten. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing the branch list, the new branch still exists; if the name is empty, contains invalid characters, ends with `/` or `.`, contains `..` or `//`, duplicates an existing name, or the user lacks write permission, an error is displayed and no branch is created. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n- REQ-4-3-2: Create a Branch from an Existing Revision - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`. The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user signs in as `alice-dev` with `Valid-password-123!` and follows the visible controls for the create a branch from an existing revision workflow. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n  - **THEN:** The page displays the required headings, controls, values, and status for the create a branch from an existing revision workflow. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing or reopening the visible destination, the successful result remains persisted; a rejected action leaves the original state unchanged. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n\n#### REQ-4-3-3 Change the Repository Default Branch\n\nA repository administrator enters “Settings” → “Branches”, selects an existing branch from the “Default branch” dropdown, clicks “Update”, and then confirms the change. The system saves the new default branch and updates the initial branch shown when the repository is newly opened; it does not delete or rewrite the previous default branch. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-4-3-1 (List and Switch Repository Branches) and REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. Only a repository Admin or organization Owner may change the default branch on repository code and version-control pages; other roles may only view. The system validates the role, stores the new default branch together with the operator and time, and rejects unauthorized requests. “Settings” and “Branches” are unique links on their respective pages; “Default branch” is a native HTML select element exposing the combobox role, with options labeled by exact existing branch names; selecting an option must use the native selection behavior. The administrator selects a different seeded branch, activates the “Update” button, and then the “Confirm” button in the confirmation dialog. Opening the repository page entry without a branch then shows “Branch <new default branch name>”; opening that selector still offers an option named exactly after the old default branch. A non-Admin may have Settings or Branches links hidden or may open a read-only settings page, but no “Default branch” combobox or default-branch update button is rendered for that user; a merely disabled editable selector is insufficient.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-default-branch-settings.png)\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-4-3-1, REQ-1-1-2\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-4-3-3: Change the Repository Default Branch - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`. A repository Admin is signed in; the repository already has `main` and `release` branches, the current default branch is `main`, and both branches retain their respective commit histories. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `release`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n  - **WHEN:** The Admin enters “Settings” → “Branches”, selects `release` from the “Default branch” dropdown, clicks “Update”, and confirms in the confirmation dialog. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `release`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n  - **THEN:** The system saves `release` as the default branch; newly opening the repository overview displays `release` by default, while the `main` branch and its commits remain available in the selector. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `release`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing the settings page, the default branch is still `release`; only existing branches can be selected, and for a non-Admin the update entry is unavailable or the request is rejected with the default branch unchanged. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n- REQ-4-3-3: Change the Repository Default Branch - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`. A signed-in user without repository-administration permission opens the Branches page in repository settings. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user attempts to view or update the default-branch setting. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n  - **THEN:** The default-branch update operation is unavailable to the non-administrator or is rejected, and the saved default branch remains unchanged. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n\n### REQ-4-4 Manage Repository Files Through the Web Interface\n\nThis feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-4-1 (Browse Repository Files and Directories) and REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. The file editor is the form opened from Edit or Add file on the Code page; the user edits file content at a path on the current branch and submits it. Only Write, Maintain, Admin, or organization Owner may submit; Read and Triage may only view. A single submission stores the file-path and content change, commit message, author, parent commit, and target branch as one indivisible record and moves the target branch to point to that new commit. A new or renamed file path must not be empty, must not begin with `/`, must not contain a `..` path segment, and must not conflict with an existing file or directory on the current branch; the commit message must contain 1–72 non-empty characters after trimming leading and trailing whitespace. If the path conflicts, content validation fails, the commit message is noncompliant, the branch is protected, or persistence fails, the page displays the reason and does not change the file, branch head, or commit history. An invalid path displays “Invalid file path”, and an empty commit message displays “Commit message is required”. From the writable Code page, the unique “Add file” button opens the “Create new file” menuitem. The editor has a field labeled “File name”, a textbox named “File contents”, an initially empty field labeled “Commit message”, and a “Commit changes” button. Creating a unique file such as `pw-file-<unique suffix>.md` with nonempty text and a message such as `Add <file name>` immediately opens a view displaying the exact saved content. Its “Commits” link opens history displaying the exact submitted message. Submitting `../invalid.md` with content `must not be saved` and no commit message reports the invalid path and/or required message and changes neither files nor history. The example content is ordinary text, not a forbidden content value by itself; rejection follows from the invalid path or missing message.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-web-file-editor.png)\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-4-1, REQ-1-1-2\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-4-4: Manage Repository Files Through the Web Interface - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`. A signed-in contributor opens the file-browser page on an unprotected branch of a writable repository; the branch-head commit is known, and the target new file path `docs/guide.md` does not yet exist. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user clicks “Add file” → “Create new file”, enters the path `docs/guide.md` and file content, enters a commit message whose trimmed length is 1 to 72, and clicks “Commit changes”. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n  - **THEN:** The system creates a new commit whose parent is the original branch head; the commit details record the author, message, and newly added path; the branch head is updated to the new commit, and the file-browser page displays the new file and its content. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing the file page and commit history, the new file and commit still exist; if the path is empty, begins with `/`, contains `..`, conflicts with an existing file, the commit message is noncompliant, the branch is protected, or persistence fails, the system displays the reason and leaves the file, branch head, and history unchanged. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n- REQ-4-4: Manage Repository Files Through the Web Interface - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`. The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user signs in as `alice-dev` with `Valid-password-123!` and follows the visible controls for the manage repository files through the web interface workflow. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n  - **THEN:** The page displays the required headings, controls, values, and status for the manage repository files through the web interface workflow. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing or reopening the visible destination, the successful result remains persisted; a rejected action leaves the original state unchanged. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.\n\n## REQ-5 Work Planning and Issue Management\n\nAn issue is a persistent work item within a repository used to record pending work, a defect, or a discussion topic; it is not a code file or PR. The Issues page is the list page in repository navigation; each row displays the issue number, title, Open/Closed status, author, and labels, and clicking the number or title opens the issue detail page. At the top of the detail page are the title and status; the right side displays Assignees, Labels, and Milestone metadata in order; the main area displays the description, comments, and activity timeline. The “activity timeline” is an append-only history that records creation, edits, comments, assignment, labels, milestone, and status changes in chronological order. A label is a pre-existing colored classification name in the current repository; a milestone is a pre-existing goal-classification item in the current repository that may be associated with multiple issues or PRs but does not change their content or status. Write, Maintain, and Admin may create issues, edit titles/descriptions, and comment; Triage, Maintain, and Admin may assign participants, apply labels, set milestones, and close or reopen issues; Read may only view. Every operation is performed around a unique issue number within the repository, and the detail page and list display the same persisted data; after refresh, re-login, or opening from another account with view permission, the same record is still read. For issue operations, the explicit role lists in this module define eligibility per operation rather than an implicit cumulative grant from a role's name; Write can create, edit, and comment but cannot assign, label, set milestones, or change status. Organization Owners act with Admin permission. Read-only seed scenarios provide a public repository's visible Issues entry, distinct Open and Closed issues with known titles, and an issue entry with known title, description, metadata, and discussion. Mutation scenarios provide isolated issues for successful editing, invalid-title editing with a known original title, commenting, comment validation, assignment, labels, milestone, and closing; their initial states must not be changed by another scenario. The author and commenter accounts have Write or higher permission; the editor used for both content and metadata operations has Maintain or Admin permission. A separate viewer has Read permission on a protected issue. All authenticated scenarios use the home-page “Sign in” link, fields labeled “Username or email” and “Password”, and the “Sign in” button, after which the signed-in username is visible. Named controls below are unique on the active page unless a narrower scope is stated; detail pages and reloads must work independently of earlier list navigation.\n\n**Type:** FOLDER\n**Dependencies:** REQ-3-3\n\n### REQ-5-1 Issue Discovery and Details\n\nThe Issues list page displays issues within the current repository; status, keyword, and label filters only change the rows currently displayed in the browser and do not write or delete issues. The issue detail page is the complete view of the work item with the same number, aggregating the description, right-side metadata, comments, and activity timeline, and also serves as the common entry point for editing, commenting, assigning, labeling, setting milestones, and closing/reopening. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-3-3 (View a Public Repository Overview); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n**Type:** FOLDER\n**Dependencies:** REQ-3-3\n\n#### REQ-5-1-1 List and Filter Repository Issues\n\nThis feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-3-3 (View a Public Repository Overview); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. Users with repository-view permission can read issue rows for the current repository on the Issues list page; each row references a persisted issue number and displays the title, status, author, labels, and update time. Users can filter by Open/Closed, title or body keywords, and labels; when the user types in the issue search box, the matching issue rows update without requiring Enter or a separate search button. Filtering does not create or modify work items. “Open” and “Closed” are links, not buttons or tabs. A unique searchbox named “Search issues” filters as the user types, including when the entire seeded title is entered. Each result title is a link whose exact accessible name is the title. Open plus the known open title shows that issue and continues to show it after reload; Closed plus the known closed title shows the closed issue and excludes the open issue. State and keyword filters combine, and refreshing retains the chosen filter context and matching results.\n\nThe seeded closed issue title is `Legacy welcome text`.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-issues-list.png)\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-3-3\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-5-1-1: List and Filter Repository Issues - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`. The user has opened the “Issues” page of an accessible repository; the list contains an Open issue with the `bug` label and a unique word in its title, and a Closed issue with the same label. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user selects “Open”, enters the unique word, and selects `bug` in the label filter. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **THEN:** The list displays only the matching Open issue, and each row displays its number, title, label, author, and status; clicking the title opens that issue's detail page. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **THEN:** After switching to “Closed”, the Open issue is not shown and the matching Closed issue is shown; filtering only changes list display and does not change any issue data. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n- REQ-5-1-1: List and Filter Repository Issues - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`. A user with repository-view permission is viewing rows containing both Open and Closed issues on the current repository's Issues list page. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **WHEN:** The participant switches to the Closed-status filter and searches for the title of a closed issue. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **THEN:** The system displays only the matching closed issue and keeps issue data unchanged after reload. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n\n#### REQ-5-1-2 View an Issue and Its Discussion\n\nAn issue detail page is the read view for a uniquely numbered work item within a repository — the top displays the number, a heading whose exact accessible name is the complete title without the issue number, and visible status text “Open” or “Closed”; the body displays the complete saved description as readable text; the right side displays assignees, labels, and milestone; and the bottom displays sections or records containing “Comment” or “Activity” text over time. Any user with repository-view permission may read this saved data, but the page displays editable controls only to users with the corresponding role. The seeded open issue title is `Improve onboarding` and its description is `Describe the onboarding improvement.` This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-5-1-1 (List and Filter Repository Issues); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-5-1-1\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-5-1-2: View an Issue and Its Discussion - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing or reopening the same number from the list, the same title, status, metadata, and saved comments are displayed; users without repository access cannot view the detail content. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n- REQ-5-1-2: View an Issue and Its Discussion - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **THEN:** The page displays the required headings, controls, values, and status for the view an issue and its discussion workflow. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing or reopening the visible destination, the successful result remains persisted; a rejected action leaves the original state unchanged. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n\n### REQ-5-2 Issue Creation and Discussion\n\nSupports creating issues in the current repository, editing titles and descriptions, and appending discussion comments. An issue title contains 1–256 non-empty characters after trimming leading and trailing whitespace; the description may be empty and is at most 65536 characters; a comment contains 1–65536 non-empty characters after trimming leading and trailing whitespace. After successful creation, the issue is assigned a unique repository-scoped number and the detail page opens; editing changes only the current fields of the target issue, while comments are appended to the activity timeline as independent records with author and timestamp. On insufficient permission, empty required fields, overlong input, or persistence failure, the system must not create an empty issue, overwrite the original description, or append a partial comment. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-5-1-2 (View an Issue and Its Discussion); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n**Type:** FOLDER\n**Dependencies:** REQ-5-1-2\n\n#### REQ-5-2-1 Create a Repository Issue\n\nThis feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-3-3 (View a Public Repository Overview) and REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. A signed-in user with Write, Maintain, or Admin clicks “New issue” from the current repository's Issues list page to open the creation form, enters a non-empty title and optional description, and submits. The system assigns an incrementing unique issue number within the repository, stores the repository identifier, title, description, author, creation time, and Open status, and appends a creation activity; on failure, it does not allocate a number or persist partial data. On success, the system redirects to the new issue detail page, and the list can locate the issue by its new number and status. “New issue” is a link on the Issues page. The creation form has fields labeled “Title” and “Description” and a “Submit new issue” button. A successful submission displays a heading named exactly after the entered title and the exact saved description; reopening the Issues page entry shows a title link with the same exact name. A title containing only three spaces is treated as blank; clicking Submit new issue displays “Title is required” and creates no issue, even if the optional description is empty.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-new-issue.png)\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-3-3, REQ-1-1-2\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-5-2-1: Create a Repository Issue - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`. A signed-in user with issue-write permission opens the repository's “Issues” page; the current maximum issue number is known, and the candidate title is not currently used on the page. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user clicks “New issue”, enters a title whose trimmed length is 1 to 256 and an optional description, then clicks “Submit new issue”. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **THEN:** The system redirects to the new issue detail page, displaying a unique incrementing repository-scoped number, Open status, the entered title and description, and the current user as author; the activity timeline contains a creation record. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **THEN:** After returning to the issue list, the item can be found by its new number; if the title is blank, exceeds 256 characters, the user lacks write permission, or submission fails, the system displays the reason and adds neither a new issue nor a new number. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n- REQ-5-2-1: Create a Repository Issue - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`. The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user signs in as `alice-dev` with `Valid-password-123!` and follows the visible controls for the create a repository issue workflow. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **THEN:** The page displays the required headings, controls, values, and status for the create a repository issue workflow. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing or reopening the visible destination, the successful result remains persisted; a rejected action leaves the original state unchanged. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n\n#### REQ-5-2-2 Edit an Issue Title and Description\n\nOnly a signed-in user with Write, Maintain, or Admin may use the unique buttons named “Edit issue title” and “Edit issue description” on an issue detail page. “Edit issue title” opens a form with a textbox labeled “Issue title” and a button named “Save issue title”; “Edit issue description” opens a form with a textbox labeled “Issue description” and a button named “Save issue description”. Read and Triage may only view, these edit controls are unavailable, and the server rejects their submissions. The system updates only the title or description of the target issue and stores the editor, edit time, and new value, displaying the change in details, list summaries, and the activity timeline; for an empty title, overlong input, or save failure, the original value is retained. Saving title and description are separate actions; after both saves and reload, the title heading has exactly the new title as its accessible name and the exact new description is visible. The invalid-edit seed is a separate issue with the original title `Original issue title`; replacing its title with three spaces and activating “Save issue title” shows “Title is required”, and reloading restores the original title heading. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-5-1-2 (View an Issue and Its Discussion); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-5-1-2\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-5-2-2: Edit an Issue Title and Description - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`. A signed-in user with edit permission opens the detail page of an issue that already has a title and description and records the issue number and original description. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user clicks “Edit” beside the title, changes the title to a valid new value and saves, then clicks “Edit” beside the description, changes the description to a valid new value and saves. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **THEN:** The title and description for the same issue number are updated, the issue-list summary displays the new title, and the activity timeline records the edits; other issues and this issue's status, labels, and assignees remain unchanged. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing the detail page, the new values still exist; saving a blank title, input over the length limit, or a failed save retains the original value of the corresponding field. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n- REQ-5-2-2: Edit an Issue Title and Description - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`. A user with edit permission opens an issue detail page whose title can be edited. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user clears the title to a blank value and attempts to save. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **THEN:** The system rejects the save, retains the original title, and continues to display the original title after reload. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n\n#### REQ-5-2-3 Comment on an Issue Discussion\n\nOnly a signed-in user with Write, Maintain, or Admin may enter a non-empty comment on the issue detail page of the current repository; all signed-in users who can view the issue may add or remove their own reactions on an existing issue or comment. The system stores the comment identifier, issue identifier, author, body, and creation time, or stores a “subject-target-reaction type” association; for the same user, target, and reaction, only one association is stored, and selecting it a second time removes it. On success, the new comment or reaction is displayed in the discussion and activity timeline; on failure, no local fake comment or partial reaction is displayed. The comment editor is labeled “Comment”, and its submit button is named exactly “Comment”. After submission the complete comment text and author username are visible, and the saved comment remains after reload. Discussion and activity entries use article semantics so an invalid submission cannot add an article; existing entries remain stable across reload. For whitespace-only text the Comment button may either be disabled or remain enabled and display “Comment is required” when activated. Neither path adds a comment or activity. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-5-1-2 (View an Issue and Its Discussion) and REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-5-1-2, REQ-1-1-2\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-5-2-3: Comment on an Issue Discussion - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`. A signed-in user with comment permission opens an issue detail page; the timeline already contains a comment to which a reaction can be added. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user enters a non-empty trimmed comment in the comment box and clicks “Comment”, then selects a reaction from the reaction menu on the existing comment. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **THEN:** The timeline appends a comment displaying the current user, comment body, and creation time; the target comment displays the reaction and its count, and the same user selecting the same reaction again does not create a duplicate reaction. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing the detail page, the comment and reaction still exist; a blank comment, a comment exceeding 65536 characters, lack of comment permission, or publication failure does not append a partial comment or timeline record. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n- REQ-5-2-3: Comment on an Issue Discussion - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`. The user opens an issue discussion page where the comment box and submit button are visible. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user enters only whitespace characters in the comment box and attempts to submit. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **THEN:** The system creates no new comment or timeline record, and the discussion count remains unchanged after reload. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n\n### REQ-5-3 Issue Metadata and Classification\n\nAn “Assignee” is an account assigned to follow up on an issue and is displayed on the right side of the detail page; it only stores an association between the issue and account and does not change that account's repository permission. A “Label” is a preconfigured classification name in the current repository and is displayed in the frontend as a label badge; it only stores an association between the issue and label. A “Milestone” is a preconfigured stage or version goal in the current repository and is displayed as single-select metadata on the right side; a work item may be associated with at most one milestone, and selecting None deletes that association. This version provides no page or interface for creating, editing, or deleting labels or milestones; seeded data and initial repositories must provide selectable items. Each relationship addition or removal updates the details and activity record and does not automatically create cross-repository labels, milestones, or membership relationships. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-5-1-2 (View an Issue and Its Discussion); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n**Type:** FOLDER\n**Dependencies:** REQ-5-1-2\n\n#### REQ-5-3-1 Assign or Unassign Issue Participants\n\nOnly a signed-in user with Triage, Maintain, or Admin may click the settings icon in the “Assignees” area on the right side of an issue, search for and check assignable members, or uncheck selected members and close the menu. Matching assignee options update as the user types in the search textbox; pressing Enter or activating a separate search button is not required before selecting an option. Assignable members are accounts that have at least Triage permission on the current repository; the system stores the issue-account relationship, operator, and time and records an activity, while non-assignable users do not appear in results. The settings icon is a button named “Assignees”; the open selector contains a textbox named “Search assignees” and items with role option named exactly after the member username. Seed data provides an eligible member not initially assigned to the target issue. Clicking the member option immediately saves the assignment and closes the selector without a separate Save action; the exact username is visible in metadata and remains after reload. Reopening Assignees shows the selected member without requiring another search; clicking that option again immediately removes the assignment and closes the selector so the username is no longer displayed as an assignee. Historical assignment and unassignment activities remain historical records. Unassignment only deletes that issue relationship and does not delete the member account or repository grant; controls are unavailable to users without the role and the server rejects their submissions. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-5-1-2 (View an Issue and Its Discussion); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-5-1-2\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-5-3-1: Assign or Unassign Issue Participants - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`. A signed-in user with issue-maintenance permission opens an issue detail page; the repository contains one assignable member and one account outside the repository collaborator scope. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user clicks the settings icon under “Assignees” on the right, searches for and checks the assignable member, closes the menu, then reopens it and unchecks that member. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **THEN:** After the member is checked, that member appears in the assignee area and an assignment activity is recorded; after unassignment, the member is no longer displayed and an unassignment activity is recorded. Non-assignable accounts do not appear in search results, and unassignment does not delete the account. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing the detail page, only the final selected-assignee set remains; users without maintenance permission cannot save assignee changes. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n\n#### REQ-5-3-2 Apply Labels to an Issue\n\nOnly users with Triage, Maintain, or Admin may apply or remove labels that already exist in the current repository; the system stores the issue-label relationship and activity record. The sidebar has a button named “Labels”, opening options whose exact accessible names are the current repository's label names. Seed data provides an existing label not yet applied to the target issue. Clicking its option immediately saves the association and closes the picker, without requiring a separate Save button; the label name is visible on the detail page and remains after reload. Reopening and selecting the same option removes the association. The label selector must not create labels, display labels from other repositories, or associate labels from other repositories; Read and Write users may only view, the control is unavailable, and the server rejects their submissions. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-5-1-2 (View an Issue and Its Discussion); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-5-1-2\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-5-3-2: Apply Labels to an Issue - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`. A signed-in user with issue-maintenance permission opens an issue detail page; the current repository defines the `bug` and `documentation` labels, while another repository contains an external label with the same name. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user opens the selector under “Labels” on the right, checks `bug`, saves, then reopens the selector and unchecks `bug`. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **THEN:** After selection, the detail page and issue list display the `bug` label; after removal, it is no longer displayed. The selector offers only labels from the current repository and does not create or associate labels from another repository. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing the page, the label set matches the last saved state; users without permission cannot submit label changes. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n\n#### REQ-5-3-3 Assign Issues and Pull Requests to a Milestone\n\nOn the right side of an issue or pull request, the user clicks the settings icon in the “Milestone” area and selects one item from the current repository's milestone list, or selects “None” to remove the association. The system stores the association between the work item and milestone and updates detail metadata; it does not create a cross-repository milestone. The settings icon is a button named “Milestone”, and selectable items have role option with the exact milestone name as their accessible name. Seed data provides a selectable milestone belonging to the current repository and an issue not yet associated with it. Clicking the option immediately saves the association and closes the picker with no separate confirmation or save action; the exact milestone name remains visible after reload. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-5-1-2 (View an Issue and Its Discussion); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. Only users with Triage, Maintain, or Admin may use this capability on repository planning and issue-management pages; Read and Write users may only view the current milestone. The system validates the role and rejects unauthorized submissions.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-milestones.png)\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-5-1-2\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-5-3-3: Assign Issues and Pull Requests to a Milestone - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session.
