Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user clicks the settings icon in “Milestone” on the right, selects `v1.0`, then reopens the selector and chooses “None”. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **THEN:** After selection, the detail page displays `v1.0` and records an association activity; after selecting “None”, that display is removed. The selector does not offer milestones from other repositories and does not create cross-repository associations. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing the detail page, the milestone state matches the last saved state; Read or Write users cannot save changes. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n\n### REQ-5-4 Close or Reopen an Issue\n\nOnly users with Triage, Maintain, or Admin may close or reopen an issue from the issue detail view; Write and Read users may only view the status. The system stores the Open or Closed status, operator, and time, records the transition in activity history, and shows it to subsequent viewers; the status transition must not modify the title, description, comments, labels, assignees, or milestone. The closable seed issue starts Open. The detail page offers a “Close issue” button when Open and a “Reopen issue” button when Closed; activating either immediately saves the change without an additional confirmation. Closing displays Closed status and a “Closed issue” activity, and reopening displays Open status. After reopening and reload the “Close issue” button is available again. On the separate protected issue, the Read viewer sees neither close nor reopen button; hiding both controls is required, in addition to rejecting unauthorized requests. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-5-1-2 (View an Issue and Its Discussion); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-5-1-2\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-5-4: Close or Reopen an Issue - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`. A signed-in user with issue-management permission opens an Open issue detail page that displays the number, title, and activity timeline. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user clicks “Close issue”, confirms that the page displays Closed, and then clicks “Reopen issue”. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **THEN:** The issue displays Closed and then Open status in sequence, and the timeline appends close and reopen events in sequence; the issue number, title, description, comments, and metadata do not change because of the status transitions. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing the detail page and issue list, the final Open state remains consistent; for users without management permission, close or reopen operations are unavailable or rejected and the status does not change. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n- REQ-5-4: Close or Reopen an Issue - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`. A signed-in user who can only view the issue and lacks issue-management permission opens the issue detail page. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user attempts to close or reopen the issue. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n  - **THEN:** Close and reopen operations are unavailable to that user or are rejected, and the issue status remains unchanged. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.\n\n## REQ-6 Change Review and Merge Control\n\nA pull request (PR) is a persistent proposal to “merge code changes from the compare branch into the base branch”; it is not a branch, commit, or issue. The Pull requests page lists the current repository's PR number, title, author, source/target branches, and status; “New pull request” first opens the comparison page, where the user selects base (the target branch that receives the changes) and compare (the source branch that provides the changes) and inspects the diff. On the PR detail page, Conversation displays the title, description, ordinary comments, activities, and review summary; Commits displays commits on the compare branch relative to base; Files changed displays per-file, per-line differences; Checks displays check status for the PR's current compare commit. A PR's persisted status may only be Draft, Open, Closed, or Merged — normal creation produces Open, draft creation produces Draft, and the author or Maintain/Admin may mark a Draft as Ready for review to transition it to Open; the author or Maintain/Admin may transition an unmerged Open or Draft PR to Closed and may transition Closed back to Open; Merged is terminal and cannot be closed or reopened. Write, Maintain, and Admin may add inline comments and submit reviews, the author or Maintain/Admin may request reviewers, and only Maintain, Admin, or organization Owner may merge. Each PR stores a repository-scoped number, source/target branches, creation-time and current compare commits, creation-time base commit, title, description, author, status, and activity records. When the compare branch receives a new commit, the system updates the current compare commit, marks all existing review decisions as stale while preserving them in the timeline, recalculates merge eligibility, and retains inline comments at their original commit and line position marked Outdated. Failure does not change branches or PR state. The following REQ-6 interaction contracts use English accessible names and apply to direct navigation as well as navigation from a repository. Each named action, field, and navigation link is unique on its active page unless explicitly repeated per changed line or reviewer. PR titles are headings whose accessible name is exactly the persisted title; list titles are links with that same exact name. Conversation, Commits, Files changed, and Checks are navigation links, even when visually styled as tabs. The current PR status is visible text. Signed-in scenarios begin with the unique Sign in link on the home page, labeled Username or email and Password fields, a Sign in button, and the account username visible after authentication; each role account has a username, verified email, and password. Visitor scenarios use public repositories and require no session. Seed records identify the corresponding pages, not to private implementation interfaces; role accounts, branches, PRs, and mutable state must be provisioned and restored independently for each scenario, including repeated and parallel runs. Existing titles, usernames, paths, and branch names described below are supplied as seed values and must be displayed verbatim; generated PR titles and comments may carry a unique suffix.\n\n**Type:** FOLDER\n**Dependencies:** REQ-3-3, REQ-4-3\n\n### REQ-6-1 Protect Branches with Review and Status-Check Requirements\n\nThis feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-4-3-1 (List and Switch Repository Branches) and REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. A “branch protection rule” is a persistent merge restriction bound to one exact branch name, displayed in the frontend under Settings → Branches, with the backend storing the branch name and rule toggles. This product supports only two independently selectable requirements: “at least 1 valid Approve from someone other than the PR author” and “required check `test` is success”. For a given reviewer on the same current compare commit, only the reviewer's latest decision among Comment, Approve, and Request changes counts. The PR author cannot satisfy the approval requirement; any valid Request changes blocks merging; when the compare branch gains a new commit, all decisions become stale and a new review is required. “Checks” is the results area on the PR detail page attached to the current compare commit; the `test` status of pending, success, or failure, together with the setter and time, must be stored, and only a repository Admin may update that status from this area. The system blocks direct writes to a protected branch and applies the rule during subsequent PR merging. The repository provides a Settings link followed by a Branches link. The rule form opens from the Add branch protection rule button and contains a field labeled Branch name pattern (an exact branch name, with no wildcard semantics), checkboxes named Require 1 approval and Require status check test, and a Create button; an existing rule uses Save changes. After saving and reloading, the branch name is visible verbatim with the summaries 1 approval and Require status check test. For a non-Admin, Add branch protection rule is absent, even if Settings or Branches remain accessible; rejecting a save alone is insufficient. On the PR detail page, the Checks area is available on arrival, displays test: pending initially, and offers the Admin a combobox named test status with a clickable option named success and a Save button. Saving displays test: success and identifies the setter and time, and reloading preserves the result for that compare commit. Seed data comprises a repository Admin and a readable non-Admin account, a dedicated repository and exact branch name with no existing rule before creation, and a separate Open PR targeting protected main with its current test check initially pending.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-branch-protection.png)\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-4-3-1, REQ-1-1-2\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-6-1: Protect Branches with Review and Status-Check Requirements - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`. A repository Admin is signed in and has opened “Settings” → “Branches”; the repository contains a `main` branch and an Open PR targeting `main`. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **WHEN:** The Admin clicks “Add branch protection rule”, enters the exact branch name `main`, checks “Require 1 approval” and “Require status check test”, and clicks “Create” or “Save changes”; the Admin then updates `test` from pending to success in the PR's “Checks” area. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** The settings page displays exactly one rule for `main` with both requirements; the PR detail page persistently displays `test: success`, the setter, and time. A PR without 1 valid non-author approval, with any valid Request changes, or with `test` not equal to success is subsequently shown as unmergeable, and the protected branch cannot be directly updated to bypass the rule. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing the settings page, the rule still exists; a non-Admin either does not see a savable rule-editing entry or has the request rejected with the rule unchanged. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n- REQ-6-1: Protect Branches with Review and Status-Check Requirements - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`. A signed-in user without repository-administration permission opens the branch-protection settings page. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user attempts to create or modify a branch protection rule. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** Branch-protection controls are unavailable to the non-administrator or the operation is rejected, and no protection rule is saved. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n- REQ-6-1: Protect Branches with Review and Status-Check Requirements - Scenario 3\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`. A repository Admin opens the detail page of an Open PR targeting protected `main`; the Checks area shows `test` as pending for the PR's current compare commit. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **WHEN:** The Admin updates `test` to success in the Checks area and saves. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** The Checks area displays success, the setter, and the update time; after refreshing the PR detail page, that status still corresponds to the same compare commit. If the compare branch gains a new commit, the new compare commit starts with pending, and success from the old commit cannot be used for merging. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n\n### REQ-6-2 Pull Request Discovery and Creation\n\nSupports listing and filtering pull requests in the current repository, selecting source/target branches to view comparison results, and creating a normal or draft pull request from a valid comparison. Read may only view; Write, Maintain, and Admin may create PRs. Comparison results are temporary read-only context on the creation page; after successful creation, the system persists the source/target branches, current compare commit, creation-time base commit, title, description, author, and status, then opens the new pull-request detail page. Creation must be rejected without leaving a partial record when source and target are the same, there are no comparable commits, the same repository already has a Draft or Open PR with the same source/target pair, or the user lacks creation permission. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-4-3 (Branch Management); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n**Type:** FOLDER\n**Dependencies:** REQ-4-3\n\n#### REQ-6-2-1 List and Filter Repository Pull Requests\n\nThe Pull requests list page only reads PR records from the current repository; each row displays the number, title, author, status, source branch, and target branch. Any user with repository-view permission may filter by Draft/Open/Closed/Merged status, author, or review status; filtering only affects the current list display and does not modify PRs, branches, or reviews. The public list is directly accessible to a visitor and has an Open link for the status filter; selecting it displays the seeded Open PR as a link whose exact accessible name is its title. Opening that link displays the same title as a heading. After reloading the filtered list, that Open PR remains visible; leaving the page and reopening the list, then selecting Open again, shows the same PR. Seed data includes this public list and a stable, uniquely titled Open PR as well as the Closed PR and author used in the filtering scenario. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-3-3 (View a Public Repository Overview); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-pull-requests-list.png)\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-3-3\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-6-2-1: List and Filter Repository Pull Requests - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** After switching to “Closed”, the Open PR is not displayed; filtering does not change the status or review data of any PR. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** The page displays the required headings, controls, values, and status for the list and filter repository pull requests workflow. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing or reopening the visible destination, the successful result remains persisted; a rejected action leaves the original state unchanged. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n\n#### REQ-6-2-2 Compare Branches Before Opening a Pull Request\n\nThe PR comparison page is a read-only page shown before creation: base is the target branch that receives the merge result, and compare is the source branch that provides the changes to merge. A user with Write, Maintain, Admin, or organization Owner status clicks “New pull request” on the “Pull requests” page and selects the two branches; the system calculates comparable commits, changed files, and a diff summary based on the commits they currently point to. If the branches are the same or there is no difference, the creation entry is disabled; comparison does not save a PR, commit, or branch change. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-4-3-1 (List and Switch Repository Branches) and REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. Only signed-in users with Write, Maintain, Admin, or organization Owner status may compare branches on the repository pull-request page; Read and Triage may view existing PRs but cannot enter the creation-comparison flow. The system validates the role and rejects unauthorized requests. New pull request is a link. The comparison page has native select controls with combobox roles named base and compare; each option's visible label is its exact branch name. Compare changes is a button. For a valid selection it displays the known changed file path verbatim, a Commit summary with the comparable commit count, and an enabled Create pull request button. Selecting the same branch in both fields immediately displays No changes and disables the creation button, without requiring Compare changes to be clicked first. Clicking Compare changes retains that result. Seed data supplies a contributor with Write permission, a public repository PR-list page entry, a base branch, a distinct compare branch ahead by at least one commit, and a known changed file. Separate valid comparison page entrys open the same usable creation flow with the branches already selected; these pairs have no existing Open or Draft PR at the start of each creation scenario.\n\nThe seeded changed-file path for comparison is `src/search.ts`.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-compare-branches.png)\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-4-3-1, REQ-1-1-2\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-6-2-2: Compare Branches Before Opening a Pull Request - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`. A signed-in user with Write, Maintain, Admin, or organization Owner status opens the “Pull requests” page; `feature-search` is one commit ahead of `main` and modifies one file. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user clicks “New pull request”, selects `main` as base and `feature-search` as compare, and clicks “Compare changes”. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** The comparison page displays both branch names, at least one comparable commit, changed files, and a diff summary, and provides an enabled “Create pull request” entry. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** After changing compare to `main`, the page explains that the branches are the same or have no differences and disables the creation entry; this read-only comparison does not create a PR or modify branches. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n- REQ-6-2-2: Compare Branches Before Opening a Pull Request - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`. A collaborator opens the comparison view on a page where base and compare branches can be selected. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **WHEN:** The collaborator selects the same branch for both base and compare and clicks Compare changes. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** The system explains that the two branches are the same or have no differences and keeps the pull-request creation entry disabled. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n\n#### REQ-6-2-3 Create a Pull Request from Comparison Results\n\nThe valid comparison page is directly accessible to the signed-in contributor. Its Create pull request button opens a form with a field labeled Title, an optional Description field, and a single Create pull request submit button; the comparison-page action is no longer a competing active button. Successful submission shows the entered title as the exact PR heading and visible Open status, and the title survives reload. A title containing only spaces is rejected with Title is required and creates no PR. A user with Write, Maintain, or Admin clicks “Create pull request” on a valid comparison-results page, enters a title containing 1–256 non-empty characters after trimming leading and trailing whitespace and an optional description of at most 65536 characters, confirms distinct base/compare branches with differences, and clicks “Create pull request”. The system atomically creates an Open PR, stores a unique repository-scoped number, source/target branches, current compare commit, current base commit, title, description, author, creation time, and activity record, and redirects to the detail page; if there is no difference, the branches are the same, a Draft or Open PR with the same source/target pair already exists, the title is empty or overlong, the user lacks permission, or submission fails, no record is created. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-6-2-2 (Compare Branches Before Opening a Pull Request); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-6-2-2\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-6-2-3: Create a Pull Request from Comparison Results - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`. A signed-in collaborator is on a valid comparison-results page for `main` and `feature-search`; the page displays at least one changed file, and no open PR currently exists for that comparison. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user clicks “Create pull request”, enters a title 1 to 256 characters long and an optional description, confirms base as `main` and compare as `feature-search`, and submits. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** The system redirects to the new PR detail page, which displays a unique repository-scoped number, Open status, source/target branches, title, description, and the current user as author. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing the detail page and PR list, the PR still exists; if the title is blank or overlong, the branches are the same, there is no difference, the user lacks creation permission, or persistence fails, no partial record is created. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n- REQ-6-2-3: Create a Pull Request from Comparison Results - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n\n#### REQ-6-2-4 Create a Draft Pull Request\n\nA visible draft comparison entry offers a Create draft pull request button, which opens the form containing Title and optional Description fields and one Create draft pull request submit button. Successful creation visibly shows Draft and a present but disabled Merge pull request button. A dedicated ready-for-review seed PR is separate from draft creation, belongs to the supplied author, initially has no submitted reviews, and displays its title, source branch, and target branch verbatim. Its Ready for review button changes the same PR to Open; if confirmation is used, it is a single Confirm button. Afterward the Draft status marker disappears, the title and branches are unchanged, and a Ready for review activity appears; Open persists on reload. A user with Write, Maintain, or Admin completes a valid base/compare branch selection, clicks “Create draft pull request”, enters a valid title and optional description, and confirms creation. The system creates a PR in Draft state using the same persisted fields as a normal PR and displays a “Draft” marker; a draft cannot submit reviews or be merged. Only the author, Maintain, Admin, or organization Owner may click “Ready for review” on a Draft detail page to convert it to Open and record an activity; this transition does not change branches, commits, title, or number. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-6-2-2 (Compare Branches Before Opening a Pull Request); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-6-2-2\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-6-2-4: Create a Draft Pull Request - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`. A signed-in collaborator is on a valid comparison page for two distinct branches with differences and has permission to create a PR. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user clicks “Create draft pull request”, enters a valid title and optional description, and confirms creation. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** The system creates a detail page displaying the “Draft” marker and source/target branches, and the PR list also shows draft status; the “Merge pull request” entry cannot be executed. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** After refresh, the draft status and content remain; if there is no valid difference or creation fails, no draft record is created. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n- REQ-6-2-4: Create a Draft Pull Request - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`. This operation does not change the PR's branches, commits, title, or description. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n\n### REQ-6-3 Pull Request Review Workspace\n\nSupports viewing the overview, commits, changed files, and diffs on a pull-request detail page, and adding review comments or submitting review decisions on specific files and line ranges in the diff. Read may view; users with Write, Maintain, or Admin who are not the PR author may comment on an Open PR and submit Comment, Approve, or Request changes; Draft does not allow review submission. An inline comment must store the target PR, compare commit, file path, line position, author, and body; a review decision must store the reviewer, current compare commit, status, and explanation. Each reviewer has only one effective decision on a given current compare commit; a new decision replaces the reviewer's effective decision while preserving history. Viewing operations are read-only, and if comment or review submission fails, it must not be displayed as published. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-6-2-3 (Create a Pull Request from Comparison Results); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n**Type:** FOLDER\n**Dependencies:** REQ-6-2-3\n\n#### REQ-6-3-1 View Pull Request Overview and Commits\n\nThe PR detail page is the unified read view for the same PR number. A seeded public Open PR is directly viewable without sign-in and displays its exact title in a heading. Commits and Files changed are links; the Commits view displays a Commit summary, and the Files changed view displays a Changed files summary. Reloading preserves the heading and usable navigation links; leaving and reopening the same visible page restores the same PR and allows the same navigation. Seed data includes its stable title, description, discussion comment, and at least one comparable commit. Conversation is the timeline for the author's description, ordinary comments, review summaries, and status events; Commits is the list of commits on the PR's current compare branch relative to base; Files changed is the entry point to the current diff. Users with repository-view permission can switch among the three tabs; all read the same persisted PR, branch, and commit data and do not create comments, reviews, or merges. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-6-2-3 (Create a Pull Request from Comparison Results); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-6-2-3\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-6-3-1: View Pull Request Overview and Commits - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`. A reviewer sees an Open PR in the PR list of an accessible repository; the PR has a title, description, at least one commit, and one discussion comment. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **WHEN:** The reviewer clicks the PR title and opens the “Conversation”, “Commits”, and “Files changed” tabs in sequence. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** Conversation displays the title, description, base/compare branches, and discussion; Commits displays the commits for the PR; Files changed displays the change summary; all three refer to the same PR number. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing any tab, the PR title, branches, and commits remain unchanged; viewing operations do not create comments, reviews, or branch updates. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n- REQ-6-3-1: View Pull Request Overview and Commits - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing or reopening the visible destination, the successful result remains persisted; a rejected action leaves the original state unchanged. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing or reopening the visible destination, the successful result remains persisted; a rejected action leaves the original state unchanged. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n\n#### REQ-6-3-2 Inspect Changed Files and Aggregate Diff\n\nFiles changed is the read-only diff view on a PR detail page computed from the current base and compare commits. From a visible public pull request entry, a visitor activates the Files changed link and sees the known changed file path verbatim and visible aggregate text such as 3 additions, 1 deletions. This public PR seed contains the same known changed-file path `src/search.ts` supplied for branch comparison, with one added file and one modified file; it requires no sign-in. Each diff block displays the file path and added/deleted lines; aggregate statistics show the current PR's number of changed files and line counts in the format “<addition count> additions, <deletion count> deletions”. Users with repository-view permission may expand files or navigate to lines, but this operation does not modify files, commits, branches, comments, or review status. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-6-3-1 (View Pull Request Overview and Commits); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-6-3-1\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-6-3-2: Inspect Changed Files and Aggregate Diff - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`. The user has opened an accessible PR detail page whose comparison contains one added file and one modified file. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user clicks “Files changed”, selects the modified file, and expands its diff block. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** The page displays the PR's changed file paths, added/deleted lines for each file, and aggregate statistics; unchanged files are not displayed, and the diff corresponds to the current source/base commits. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing or switching back to Conversation, the files, PR status, and review status remain unchanged; users without permission to view a private PR cannot obtain its diff content. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n\n#### REQ-6-3-3 Add Review Comments to Changed Code Lines\n\nAn inline review comment is a comment record anchored to a file path, the current compare commit, and a specific added/deleted line in Files changed; it differs from an ordinary comment in Conversation that has no code location. A user with Write, Maintain, or Admin who is not the PR author can hover over a changed line, click the button visually shown as “+” and accessibly named Add comment, and choose “Add single comment” to publish immediately or “Start a review” to keep it as a pending review draft. The changed-line Add comment buttons are available for direct activation without a prerequisite hover; the first such button in document order targets the first commentable changed line. Activating one opens a single editor labeled Comment with Add single comment and Start a review buttons. Add single comment immediately displays the exact entered body in that diff view and retains it after reload. Start a review displays the body and Pending review to its author immediately, retains the pending draft after reload, and does not make it public before review submission. Seed data supplies a non-author Write reviewer, an Open reviewable PR with at least one changed line, and a separate Open PR for the pending-comment scenario. The system stores the PR, file path, compare commit, line position, author, body, and publication state; when the compare commit changes, published comments are retained but marked Outdated, while drafts are not published automatically. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-6-3-2 (Inspect Changed Files and Aggregate Diff) and REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-6-3-2, REQ-1-1-2\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-6-3-3: Add Review Comments to Changed Code Lines - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`. A signed-in reviewer opens the “Files changed” page of a PR they are allowed to comment on and locates an added code line and its file path. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **WHEN:** The reviewer hovers over the added line and clicks “+”, enters a non-empty inline comment, and selects “Add single comment”. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** Conversation and the diff page immediately display the comment; the comment record is associated with the current PR, file path, diff commit, and target line position and displays the reviewer and body. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** After refresh, the comment remains anchored to that file and line; when “Start a review” is selected, the comment remains pending and is not public until the review is submitted, while an empty comment or failed submission does not display a partial comment. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n- REQ-6-3-3: Add Review Comments to Changed Code Lines - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`. The reviewer locates a changed line in the pull request's Files changed view. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **WHEN:** The reviewer enters an inline comment and selects Start a review instead of Add single comment. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** The system keeps the comment in a pending state, does not publish it immediately, and displays it only after the review is submitted. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n\n#### REQ-6-3-4 Submit a Pull Request Review\n\nFrom the Files changed link, the reviewer activates the Review changes button to open one review form with an optional Summary field, radio controls named Comment, Approve, and Request changes, and a Submit review button. Selecting Approve and submitting without a summary is valid and displays Approved. Selecting Request changes with a summary displays Changes requested and that exact summary; the decision remains visible after reload. Seed data supplies a non-author Write reviewer and separate Open PRs for approval and request-changes submission, with no initial effective decision by that reviewer. A user who is not the PR author and has Write, Maintain, or Admin may submit Comment, Approve, or Request changes on an Open PR; the author and Draft PRs cannot submit reviews. The system stores the review decision, reviewer, current compare commit, optional explanation, and time; a new decision by the same reviewer replaces that reviewer's effective decision for the current commit while preserving the old record. Approve is valid only when the reviewer is not the author and the compare commit is still the same; Request changes blocks merging until that reviewer submits a new Comment or Approve for the current compare commit, or the compare branch receives a new commit that makes the old decision stale. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-6-3-1 (View Pull Request Overview and Commits) and REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-6-3-1, REQ-1-1-2\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-6-3-4: Submit a Pull Request Review - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`. A signed-in reviewer opens an Open PR that can be reviewed, and the PR currently has no “Approved” decision from that reviewer. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **WHEN:** The reviewer clicks “Review changes” under “Files changed”, enters an overall comment, selects “Approve”, and clicks “Submit review”. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** Conversation and the review summary on the right display the reviewer, Approved status, overall comment, and time; branch-protection merge-eligibility checks can read this decision. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** After refresh, the decision still exists; selecting “Request changes” displays the corresponding status, while a user without review permission or a failed submission does not persist a decision. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n- REQ-6-3-4: Submit a Pull Request Review - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`. A reviewer opens a pull request that can be reviewed. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **WHEN:** The reviewer enters review feedback, selects Request changes, and submits the review. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** The system saves the review comment and changes requested status and continues to display the decision after reload. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n\n### REQ-6-4 Request or Remove Pull Request Reviewers\n\nThe Reviewers button opens a picker with a textbox named Search. Typing an eligible username immediately reveals an option with that exact accessible name. Selecting the option saves the request immediately without a separate Save action, closes the picker, and displays the username in the reviewer area; it remains after reload. Each requested reviewer has a button named Remove followed by a space and the username; clicking it immediately removes the request and the username from that area, without a confirmation step. The seed PR is Open, belongs to the signed-in author, and initially has no request or submitted review from the eligible target reviewer; the target is a distinct non-author collaborator with Write or higher permission. Removing a request persists across reload. A “reviewer request” is a pending-review relationship between a PR and a candidate reviewer account, displayed in the Reviewers area on the right side of the PR detail page; it is not the same as a submitted review decision. The author of an Open or Draft PR, Maintain, Admin, or organization Owner may create or delete this relationship. Matching reviewer options update as the user types in the search textbox; pressing Enter or activating a separate search button is not required before selecting an option. A candidate reviewer must have Write, Maintain, or Admin on the repository and must not be the PR author; the system stores or deletes the “PR-reviewer request” relationship together with the operator and time. Removing a request does not delete reviews, comments, or activity records already submitted by that user and does not change the user's effective review decision. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-6-2-3 (Create a Pull Request from Comparison Results) and REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-6-2-3, REQ-1-1-2\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-6-4: Request or Remove Pull Request Reviewers - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The PR author or a maintainer is signed in and has opened an Open PR detail page; the repository contains a collaborator who is eligible to be requested as a reviewer. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user searches for and selects the collaborator under “Reviewers” on the right, saves, and then clicks the remove button beside that collaborator. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** After selection, the account is shown on the right as a requested reviewer; after removal, the request disappears; this does not automatically generate an approval or comment. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing the detail page, only the final request set remains; a user who is neither the author nor a maintainer cannot modify requests. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n\n### REQ-6-5 Merge an Eligible Pull Request\n\nOn an eligible PR, Merge pull request and then Confirm merge are buttons; confirming displays Merged, and that state remains after reload. A blocked PR keeps a visible disabled Merge pull request button and explains its unmet review or protection condition before any click. Seed data supplies a Maintain account and separate Open PRs for success and refusal. The eligible PR targets protected main, has a current non-author approval and test: success, and has no conflicts. The blocked PR targets a protected branch and lacks the required valid approval; its disabled button is accompanied by Review required by branch protection. Each seed state is restored independently before reuse. “Merge” is the only write operation that writes the current compare commit's changes from an Open PR into the base branch; it is not simply marking the PR as complete. Only Maintain, Admin, or organization Owner may merge from the PR detail page using the sole supported “Create a merge commit” method; the merge confirmation area should display satisfied and unsatisfied conditions. Before confirmation, the system rereads the target branch head, current compare commit, merge-conflict state, and protection rules — there must be no valid Request changes; if the target is protected, each enabled rule requirement is enforced independently, requiring 1 valid non-author Approve for the current compare commit when Require 1 approval is enabled and requiring `test` success when Require status check test is enabled. An unprotected target has no additional approval-count or check-success requirement, but merging still requires no merge conflicts and no valid Request changes review. After validation succeeds, the system atomically creates a merge commit whose parents are the target-branch head at merge time and the current compare commit, updates the target-branch head, sets the PR to Merged, and stores the merger, time, and resulting commit identifier. If any condition is unsatisfied or persistence fails, the page explains the applicable reason and neither the target branch nor PR changes; a missing required approval displays “Review required by branch protection”. Merged is terminal. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-6-3-4 (Submit a Pull Request Review) and REQ-6-1 (Protect Branches with Review and Status-Check Requirements); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-6-3-4, REQ-6-1\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-6-5: Merge an Eligible Pull Request - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`. A signed-in user with Maintain, Admin, or organization Owner status opens an Open, non-draft PR; its target `main` branch is protected, it has 1 valid non-author Approve for the current compare commit, `test` is success in Checks, and the current diff has no merge conflict. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **WHEN:** The maintainer confirms that the only selectable method on the page is “Create a merge commit”, clicks “Merge pull request”, and clicks “Confirm merge” in the confirmation box. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** The PR displays Merged, the merger, time, and resulting commit identifier; the `main` branch head is updated to the merge result, and the PR's source- and target-branch content is integrated according to the selected merge method. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing the PR and `main` branch, the merged status and resulting commit remain; if the PR is a draft, lacks approval, has a failed check, is unmergeable, or the user lacks permission, the merge entry is disabled or rejected and the target branch and PR state remain unchanged. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n- REQ-6-5: Merge an Eligible Pull Request - Scenario 2\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The seeded data is pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`. The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user signs in as `alice-dev` with `Valid-password-123!` and follows the visible controls for the merge an eligible pull request workflow. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** The page displays the required headings, controls, values, and status for the merge an eligible pull request workflow. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing or reopening the visible destination, the successful result remains persisted; a rejected action leaves the original state unchanged. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n\n### REQ-6-6 Close or Reopen a Pull Request Without Merging\n\nThe PR page provides Close pull request and Reopen pull request as buttons for authorized users. Close pull request immediately sets Closed without an extra confirmation dialog, then Reopen pull request immediately restores Open. After reload, Close pull request is available again. For a viewer who is neither author nor Maintain/Admin/Owner, both controls are absent, rather than merely disabled or rejected after activation. Seed data supplies the author and a separate Read viewer, an authored Open PR for the close/reopen cycle, and a separate Open PR readable by that viewer for permission validation. When the proposed change should not proceed, the PR author, Maintain, Admin, or organization Owner may close an unmerged Open or Draft PR without merging; the same roles may reopen a Closed PR as Open. The system stores the status, operator, and time and records the transition, while keeping the PR discussion, reviews, diff, and branch references viewable; closing or reopening must not update any branch. Merged is terminal, the page does not display close or reopen operations, and the server rejects status changes. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-6-2-3 (Create a Pull Request from Comparison Results) and REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n**Type:** ATOMIC\n**Dependencies:** REQ-6-2-3, REQ-1-1-2\n\n**Scenarios:**\n\n- REQ-6-6: Close or Reopen a Pull Request Without Merging - Scenario 1\n  - **GIVEN:** The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **WHEN:** The user clicks “Close pull request”, confirms that the status is Closed, and then clicks “Reopen pull request”. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** The PR displays Closed and Open in sequence, and the timeline records both status transitions; closing does not merge commits, update the target branch, or delete the discussion or diff. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.\n  - **THEN:** After refreshing the detail page, the final Open status and original discussion remain; a viewer who is neither the author nor a maintainer cannot close or reopen the PR, and the PR status remains unchanged. The seeded data is pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.     "requirements_yaml": "id: ROOT\nname: GitHub Collaboration Platform Core Requirements\ntype: FOLDER\ndependencies: []\ndescription: This product is a simplified GitHub collaboration platform. Unless an\n  atomic requirement explicitly allows more than one role, every quoted user-interface\n  name is the exact English accessible name of the control; the role, scope, and observable-state\n  rules stated by each atomic requirement are authoritative. Values described as existing\n  accounts, organizations, teams, repositories, branches, files, commits, issues,\n  milestones, pull requests, reviews, and permission relationships are predefined\n  seed data. The application must provision those records before the corresponding\n  scenario and supply each account through an isolated browser session; they are not\n  extra features that a participant must expose through a private API. Generated\n  names may contain a unique suffix, but their role, ownership, visibility, and relationships\n  are exactly those stated in the atomic requirement. Every atomic requirement remains\n  enabled even when seed configuration is incomplete. All write operations\n  must be persisted on the server, checked against the current session and target-object\n  permission, and completed atomically. Organization Owner, repository Admin, Read,\n  Triage, Write, Maintain, and Admin permissions follow the operation-specific rules\n  in the requirements below; these roles are not an automatic cumulative ladder. Real-time\n  collaboration, Actions, Packages, Wiki, project boards, notification delivery, and\n  external Git remote protocols are outside the scope of this product.\nchildren:\n- id: REQ-1\n  name: Identity and Access\n  type: FOLDER\n  dependencies: []\n  description: This module defines the “account” and “session” required to access\n    the system. An account is a persistent individual identity storing a username,\n    verified email, and credential status; a session is a record created after successful\n    account sign-in that represents only the current browser login state. Visitors\n    enter the account-access page from “Sign up”, “Sign in”, or “Forgot password”\n    on the home page; signed-in users enter security settings or sign out from the\n    account menu. The account menu is the control in the upper-right corner of the\n    page that displays the current signed-in account, organization list, and sign-out\n    entry. No page, error message, or recovery flow may display a full password. After\n    successful sign-in, the account menu displays the current user, and subsequent\n    organization, repository, issue, and pull-request pages use this session to determine\n    visibility and operation permissions; sign-out, password changes, and recovery\n    must immediately change the session state visible to subsequent requests.\n  children:\n  - id: REQ-1-1\n    name: Account Registration and Recovery\n    type: FOLDER\n    dependencies: []\n    description: The account-access page is the shared page used by unauthenticated\n      users to register, sign in, and recover a password, with the frontend showing\n      the appropriate form and submit button for each operation. The local product\n      does not implement an email-verification page, email delivery, or an external\n      verification-code service — successful registration directly stores a sign-in-capable\n      account and marks its email as verified; successful sign-in only stores a new\n      session and does not create a duplicate account; after submitting an email for\n      password recovery, the page directly displays the fixed verification code `123456`\n      and the password-reset form, and only the correct code together with a compliant\n      new password updates the password of the same account. Registration, sign-in,\n      and recovery pages must use visible labels to identify fields and submit actions;\n      after failure, non-sensitive input is retained, while password and password-confirmation\n      fields must not redisplay submitted values.\n    children:\n    - id: REQ-1-1-1\n      name: Register a New GitHub Account\n      type: ATOMIC\n      dependencies: []\n      description: |-\n        The registration page is the form opened by the unique link named “Create an account” from the sign-in page. The form contains exactly one textbox labeled “Username”, one textbox labeled “Email”, one password input labeled “Password”, one password input labeled “Confirm password”, one initially unchecked checkbox named “Agree to the terms”, and one enabled button named “Create account”. The username and email identify the account, while passwords must not be echoed on the page. This product directly marks the email of a successfully registered account as verified and does not provide a subsequent email-verification page. The visitor enters a unique username, email, password, and matching confirmation, checks “Agree to the terms”, and activates “Create account”. The username must consist of 1 to 39 lowercase ASCII letters, digits, or single hyphens (`-`) and must not begin or end with a hyphen; after trimming leading and trailing whitespace, the email must contain exactly one `@`, be no longer than 254 characters, and have at least one dot and non-empty domain labels after the `@`; the password must contain 12 to 128 characters, include at least one uppercase letter, one lowercase letter, one digit, and one non-alphanumeric special character, and contain no whitespace characters. The system stores a sign-in-capable account together with its verified-email status and enters the sign-in page; if the username or email conflicts, any field is missing, the email format is invalid, the terms are not accepted, the password is noncompliant, or the confirmation does not match, the system retains the username and email input, displays an itemized message beside the corresponding field, and does not create an account. The corresponding visible field message contains “Username already exists”, “Username format is invalid”, “Email format is invalid”, “Password requirements are not satisfied”, or “Agree to terms is required”, as applicable. Visitors can use this capability on the account-access page. The system validates input rules, displays field errors, and prevents duplicate accounts; on success, it directly creates an available account. Submitting several invalid fields together must show the username, email, password, and missing-terms messages together, rather than revealing only one error per submission; the submit button remains actionable so these messages can be read. For example, a username beginning with a hyphen, email “not-an-email”, password “short”, confirmation “different”, and unchecked terms produce these field errors and preserve the attempted username. A duplicate username paired with a different unused email displays “Username already exists” and retains both attempted values. A successful registration accepts a unique username such as “pw-user-<unique suffix>”, its “@example.test” email, and password “Valid-password-123!”; the sign-in form is immediately available, accepts that email, and the resulting signed-in username remains visible after reload.\n\n        Screenshot reference:\n        ![image](reference/github-sign-up.png)\n      scenarios:\n      - name: 'REQ-1-1-1: Register a New GitHub Account - Scenario 1'\n        steps:\n        - keyword: GIVEN\n          content: 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated\n            browser session. Returning with browser Back keeps the public result available; filtering by the exact private repository name never exposes its link to a visitor. The seed organization has a public repository and a distinct private repository inaccessible to visitors. From the organization page, the user clicks the “Repositories” link, can enter a repository name in the filter box, select a public/private filter, and click a repository name in the list to open its overview. The system displays only the names, descriptions, visibility, and update times of repositories visible to the current user; clicking a result opens that repository's overview. Visitors can view public repositories on a public organization overview page; signed-in accounts can see only repositories for which they have read permission on the Repositories tab of the organization overview. The list reads repository name, description, visibility, and update time and provides no write operations; private repositories must first be validated under the already defined repository-access rules.\n\n        Screenshot reference:\n        ![image](reference/github-organization-repositories.png)\n      scenarios:\n      - name: 'REQ-2-1-1: Browse Organization Repositories - Scenario 1'\n        steps:\n        - keyword: GIVEN\n          content: 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated\n            browser session. This feature\n        builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context\n        provided by REQ-2-1-2 (Create an Organization After Authentication); it must\n        reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n      scenarios:\n      - name: 'REQ-2-2-4: Remove a Member from an Organization - Scenario 1'\n        steps:\n        - keyword: GIVEN\n          content: 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated\n            browser session. A signed-in user can fork only when they have Read or higher permission on the source repository and permission to create repositories in the target personal or organization namespace; when the source repository is private, the fork must remain Private, while a public source repository may be forked as Public or Private. Creating a branch creates a new named reference at a\n      specified base commit; it does not copy files or rewrite base history. The default\n      branch is the branch read by default when opening the repository overview or\n      a repository entry without a specified branch; it is not the only existing or writable branch.\n      A branch name is 1–255 characters and may contain only ASCII letters, digits,\n      `-`, `_`, `.`, and `/`; it must not end with `/` or `.`, and must not contain\n      consecutive `..` or `//`; duplicate names, invalid names, or lack of permission\n      must prevent branch creation. The system creates a new branch pointing to that base commit and switches the selector to it; if the name already exists or the user lacks permission, no branch is created. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-4-3-1 (List and Switch Repository Branches) and REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. Only signed-in users with Write, Maintain, Admin, or organization Owner status may create branches on repository code and version-control pages; Read and Triage may only browse. The system validates the role, stores the new branch name, base commit, creator, and time, and rejects unauthorized requests. The selector and “Find branch” textbox use REQ-4-3-1's roles. Its displayed base defaults to the current branch head; selecting this option performs creation and switches branches without a second mandatory confirmation. Reloading the resulting page entry keeps the newly created branch selected. The system saves the new default branch and updates the initial branch shown when the repository is newly opened; it does not delete or rewrite the previous default branch. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-4-3-1 (List and Switch Repository Branches) and REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. Only a repository Admin or organization Owner may change the default branch on repository code and version-control pages; other roles may only view. The system validates the role, stores the new default branch together with the operator and time, and rejects unauthorized requests. “Settings” and “Branches” are unique links on their respective pages; “Default branch” is a native HTML select element exposing the combobox role, with options labeled by exact existing branch names; selecting an option must use the native selection behavior. The administrator selects a different seeded branch, activates the “Update” button, and then the “Confirm” button in the confirmation dialog. Opening the repository page entry without a branch then shows “Branch <new default branch name>”; opening that selector still offers an option named exactly after the old default branch. A non-Admin may have Settings or Branches links hidden or may open a read-only settings page, but no “Default branch” combobox or default-branch update button is rendered for that user; a merely disabled editable selector is insufficient.\n\n        Screenshot reference:\n        ![image](reference/github-default-branch-settings.png)\n      scenarios:\n      - name: 'REQ-4-3-3: Change the Repository Default Branch - Scenario 1'\n        steps:\n        - keyword: GIVEN\n          content: 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated\n            browser session. The file editor is the form opened from Edit or Add file on the Code page; the user edits file content at a path on the current branch and submits it. Only Write, Maintain, Admin, or organization Owner may submit; Read and Triage may only view. A single submission stores the file-path and content change, commit message, author, parent commit, and target branch as one indivisible record and moves the target branch to point to that new commit. A new or renamed file path must not be empty, must not begin with `/`, must not contain a `..` path segment, and must not conflict with an existing file or directory on the current branch; the commit message must contain 1–72 non-empty characters after trimming leading and trailing whitespace. If the path conflicts, content validation fails, the commit message is noncompliant, the branch is protected, or persistence fails, the page displays the reason and does not change the file, branch head, or commit history. An invalid path displays “Invalid file path”, and an empty commit message displays “Commit message is required”. From the writable Code page, the unique “Add file” button opens the “Create new file” menuitem. The editor has a field labeled “File name”, a textbox named “File contents”, an initially empty field labeled “Commit message”, and a “Commit changes” button. Creating a unique file such as `pw-file-<unique suffix>.md` with nonempty text and a message such as `Add <file name>` immediately opens a view displaying the exact saved content. Its “Commits” link opens history displaying the exact submitted message. Submitting `../invalid.md` with content `must not be saved` and no commit message reports the invalid path and/or required message and changes neither files nor history. The example content is ordinary text, not a forbidden content value by itself; rejection follows from the invalid path or missing message.\n\n      Screenshot reference:\n      ![image](reference/github-web-file-editor.png)\n    scenarios:\n    - name: 'REQ-4-4: Manage Repository Files Through the Web Interface - Scenario\n        1'\n      steps:\n      - keyword: GIVEN\n        content: 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated\n          browser session. The\n    Issues page is the list page in repository navigation; each row displays the issue\n    number, title, Open/Closed status, author, and labels, and clicking the number\n    or title opens the issue detail page. At the top of the detail page are the title\n    and status; the right side displays Assignees, Labels, and Milestone metadata\n    in order; the main area displays the description, comments, and activity timeline.\n    The “activity timeline” is an append-only history that records creation, edits,\n    comments, assignment, labels, milestone, and status changes in chronological order.\n    A label is a pre-existing colored classification name in the current repository;\n    a milestone is a pre-existing goal-classification item in the current repository\n    that may be associated with multiple issues or PRs but does not change their content\n    or status. Write, Maintain, and Admin may create issues, edit titles/descriptions,\n    and comment; Triage, Maintain, and Admin may assign participants, apply labels,\n    set milestones, and close or reopen issues; Read may only view. Every operation\n    is performed around a unique issue number within the repository, and the detail\n    page and list display the same persisted data; after refresh, re-login, or opening\n    from another account with view permission, the same record is still read. For\n    issue operations, the explicit role lists in this module define eligibility per\n    operation rather than an implicit cumulative grant from a role's name; Write can\n    create, edit, and comment but cannot assign, label, set milestones, or change\n    status. Organization Owners act with Admin permission. Read-only seed scenarios\n    provide a public repository's visible Issues entry, distinct Open and\n    Closed issues with known titles, and an issue entry with known title, description,\n    metadata, and discussion. Mutation scenarios provide isolated issues for successful\n    editing, invalid-title editing with a known original title, commenting, comment\n    validation, assignment, labels, milestone, and closing; their initial states must\n    not be changed by another scenario. The author and commenter accounts have Write\n    or higher permission; the editor used for both content and metadata operations\n    has Maintain or Admin permission. A separate viewer has Read permission on a protected\n    issue. All authenticated scenarios use the home-page “Sign in” link, fields labeled\n    “Username or email” and “Password”, and the “Sign in” button, after which the\n    signed-in username is visible. Named controls below are unique on the active page\n    unless a narrower scope is stated; detail pages and reloads must work independently\n    of earlier list navigation.\n  children:\n  - id: REQ-5-1\n    name: Issue Discovery and Details\n    type: FOLDER\n    dependencies:\n    - REQ-3-3\n    description: The Issues list page displays issues within the current repository;\n      status, keyword, and label filters only change the rows currently displayed\n      in the browser and do not write or delete issues.
