An issue title contains\n      1–256 non-empty characters after trimming leading and trailing whitespace; the\n      description may be empty and is at most 65536 characters; a comment contains\n      1–65536 non-empty characters after trimming leading and trailing whitespace.\n      After successful creation, the issue is assigned a unique repository-scoped\n      number and the detail page opens; editing changes only the current fields of\n      the target issue, while comments are appended to the activity timeline as independent\n      records with author and timestamp. On insufficient permission, empty required\n      fields, overlong input, or persistence failure, the system must not create an\n      empty issue, overwrite the original description, or append a partial comment.\n      This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session\n      context provided by REQ-5-1-2 (View an Issue and Its Discussion); it must reuse\n      that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n    children:\n    - id: REQ-5-2-1\n      name: Create a Repository Issue\n      type: ATOMIC\n      dependencies:\n      - REQ-3-3\n      - REQ-1-1-2\n      description: |-\n        This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-3-3 (View a Public Repository Overview) and REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. A signed-in user with Write, Maintain, or Admin clicks “New issue” from the current repository's Issues list page to open the creation form, enters a non-empty title and optional description, and submits. The system assigns an incrementing unique issue number within the repository, stores the repository identifier, title, description, author, creation time, and Open status, and appends a creation activity; on failure, it does not allocate a number or persist partial data. On success, the system redirects to the new issue detail page, and the list can locate the issue by its new number and status. “New issue” is a link on the Issues page. The creation form has fields labeled “Title” and “Description” and a “Submit new issue” button. A successful submission displays a heading named exactly after the entered title and the exact saved description; reopening the Issues page entry shows a title link with the same exact name. A title containing only three spaces is treated as blank; clicking Submit new issue displays “Title is required” and creates no issue, even if the optional description is empty.\n\n        Screenshot reference:\n        ![image](reference/github-new-issue.png)\n      scenarios:\n      - name: 'REQ-5-2-1: Create a Repository Issue - Scenario 1'\n        steps:\n        - keyword: GIVEN\n          content: 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated\n            browser session. A “Label” is a preconfigured classification name in the current\n      repository and is displayed in the frontend as a label badge; it only stores\n      an association between the issue and label. A “Milestone” is a preconfigured\n      stage or version goal in the current repository and is displayed as single-select\n      metadata on the right side; a work item may be associated with at most one milestone,\n      and selecting None deletes that association. This version provides no page or\n      interface for creating, editing, or deleting labels or milestones; seeded data\n      and initial repositories must provide selectable items. Each relationship addition\n      or removal updates the details and activity record and does not automatically\n      create cross-repository labels, milestones, or membership relationships. This\n      feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session\n      context provided by REQ-5-1-2 (View an Issue and Its Discussion); it must reuse\n      that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n    children:\n    - id: REQ-5-3-1\n      name: Assign or Unassign Issue Participants\n      type: ATOMIC\n      dependencies:\n      - REQ-5-1-2\n      description: Only a signed-in user with Triage, Maintain, or Admin may click\n        the settings icon in the “Assignees” area on the right side of an issue, search\n        for and check assignable members, or uncheck selected members and close the\n        menu. Matching assignee options update as the user types in the search textbox;\n        pressing Enter or activating a separate search button is not required before\n        selecting an option. The settings icon is a button named “Assignees”;\n        the open selector contains a textbox named “Search assignees” and items with\n        role option named exactly after the member username. Seed data provides an\n        eligible member not initially assigned to the target issue. Clicking the member\n        option immediately saves the assignment and closes the selector without a\n        separate Save action; the exact username is visible in metadata and remains\n        after reload. Reopening Assignees shows the selected member without requiring\n        another search; clicking that option again immediately removes the assignment\n        and closes the selector so the username is no longer displayed as an assignee.\n        Historical assignment and unassignment activities remain historical records.\n        Unassignment only deletes that issue relationship and does not delete the\n        member account or repository grant; controls are unavailable to users without\n        the role and the server rejects their submissions. The Pull requests page lists the current repository's PR number, title,\n    author, source/target branches, and status; “New pull request” first opens the\n    comparison page, where the user selects base (the target branch that receives\n    the changes) and compare (the source branch that provides the changes) and inspects\n    the diff. On the PR detail page, Conversation displays the title, description,\n    ordinary comments, activities, and review summary; Commits displays commits on\n    the compare branch relative to base; Files changed displays per-file, per-line\n    differences; Checks displays check status for the PR's current compare commit.\n    A PR's persisted status may only be Draft, Open, Closed, or Merged — normal creation\n    produces Open, draft creation produces Draft, and the author or Maintain/Admin\n    may mark a Draft as Ready for review to transition it to Open; the author or Maintain/Admin\n    may transition an unmerged Open or Draft PR to Closed and may transition Closed\n    back to Open; Merged is terminal and cannot be closed or reopened. Write, Maintain,\n    and Admin may add inline comments and submit reviews, the author or Maintain/Admin\n    may request reviewers, and only Maintain, Admin, or organization Owner may merge.\n    Each PR stores a repository-scoped number, source/target branches, creation-time\n    and current compare commits, creation-time base commit, title, description, author,\n    status, and activity records. When the compare branch receives a new commit, the\n    system updates the current compare commit, marks all existing review decisions\n    as stale while preserving them in the timeline, recalculates merge eligibility,\n    and retains inline comments at their original commit and line position marked\n    Outdated. Failure does not change branches or PR state. The following REQ-6 interaction\n    contracts use English accessible names and apply to direct navigation as well\n    as navigation from a repository. Each named action, field, and navigation link\n    is unique on its active page unless explicitly repeated per changed line or reviewer.\n    PR titles are headings whose accessible name is exactly the persisted title; list\n    titles are links with that same exact name. Conversation, Commits, Files changed,\n    and Checks are navigation links, even when visually styled as tabs. The current\n    PR status is visible text. Signed-in scenarios begin with the unique Sign in link\n    on the home page, labeled Username or email and Password fields, a Sign in button,\n    and the account username visible after authentication; each role account has a\n    username, verified email, and password. Visitor scenarios use public repositories\n    and require no session. Seed records identify the corresponding pages, not to\n    private implementation interfaces; role accounts, branches, PRs, and mutable state must\n    be provisioned and restored independently for each scenario, including repeated\n    and parallel runs. Existing titles, usernames, paths, and branch names described\n    below are supplied as seed values and must be displayed verbatim; generated\n    PR titles and comments may carry a unique suffix.\n  children:\n  - id: REQ-6-1\n    name: Protect Branches with Review and Status-Check Requirements\n    type: ATOMIC\n    dependencies:\n    - REQ-4-3-1\n    - REQ-1-1-2\n    description: |-\n      This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-4-3-1 (List and Switch Repository Branches) and REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. A “branch protection rule” is a persistent merge restriction bound to one exact branch name, displayed in the frontend under Settings → Branches, with the backend storing the branch name and rule toggles. This product supports only two independently selectable requirements: “at least 1 valid Approve from someone other than the PR author” and “required check `test` is success”. For a given reviewer on the same current compare commit, only the reviewer's latest decision among Comment, Approve, and Request changes counts. The PR author cannot satisfy the approval requirement; any valid Request changes blocks merging; when the compare branch gains a new commit, all decisions become stale and a new review is required. “Checks” is the results area on the PR detail page attached to the current compare commit; the `test` status of pending, success, or failure, together with the setter and time, must be stored, and only a repository Admin may update that status from this area. The system blocks direct writes to a protected branch and applies the rule during subsequent PR merging. The repository provides a Settings link followed by a Branches link. The rule form opens from the Add branch protection rule button and contains a field labeled Branch name pattern (an exact branch name, with no wildcard semantics), checkboxes named Require 1 approval and Require status check test, and a Create button; an existing rule uses Save changes. After saving and reloading, the branch name is visible verbatim with the summaries 1 approval and Require status check test. For a non-Admin, Add branch protection rule is absent, even if Settings or Branches remain accessible; rejecting a save alone is insufficient. On the PR detail page, the Checks area is available on arrival, displays test: pending initially, and offers the Admin a combobox named test status with a clickable option named success and a Save button. Saving displays test: success and identifies the setter and time, and reloading preserves the result for that compare commit. Seed data comprises a repository Admin and a readable non-Admin account, a dedicated repository and exact branch name with no existing rule before creation, and a separate Open PR targeting protected main with its current test check initially pending.\n\n      Screenshot reference:\n      ![image](reference/github-branch-protection.png)\n    scenarios:\n    - name: 'REQ-6-1: Protect Branches with Review and Status-Check Requirements -\n        Scenario 1'\n      steps:\n      - keyword: GIVEN\n        content: 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated\n          browser session. A user with Write, Maintain, Admin, or organization Owner status clicks “New pull request” on the “Pull requests” page and selects the two branches; the system calculates comparable commits, changed files, and a diff summary based on the commits they currently point to. If the branches are the same or there is no difference, the creation entry is disabled; comparison does not save a PR, commit, or branch change. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-4-3-1 (List and Switch Repository Branches) and REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. Only signed-in users with Write, Maintain, Admin, or organization Owner status may compare branches on the repository pull-request page; Read and Triage may view existing PRs but cannot enter the creation-comparison flow. The system validates the role and rejects unauthorized requests. New pull request is a link. The comparison page has native select controls with combobox roles named base and compare; each option's visible label is its exact branch name. Compare changes is a button. For a valid selection it displays the known changed file path verbatim, a Commit summary with the comparable commit count, and an enabled Create pull request button. Selecting the same branch in both fields immediately displays No changes and disables the creation button, without requiring Compare changes to be clicked first. Clicking Compare changes retains that result. Seed data supplies a contributor with Write permission, a public repository PR-list page entry, a base branch, a distinct compare branch ahead by at least one commit, and a known changed file. Separate valid comparison page entrys open the same usable creation flow with the branches already selected; these pairs have no existing Open or Draft PR at the start of each creation scenario.\n\n        The seeded changed-file path for comparison is `src/search.ts`.\n\n        Screenshot reference:\n        ![image](reference/github-compare-branches.png)\n      scenarios:\n      - name: 'REQ-6-2-2: Compare Branches Before Opening a Pull Request - Scenario\n          1'\n        steps:\n        - keyword: GIVEN\n          content: 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated\n            browser session. Seed values: pull\n            requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`,\n            reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.'\n  - id: REQ-6-4\n    name: Request or Remove Pull Request Reviewers\n    type: ATOMIC\n    dependencies:\n    - REQ-6-2-3\n    - REQ-1-1-2\n    description: The Reviewers button opens a picker with a textbox named Search.\n      Typing an eligible username immediately reveals an option with that exact accessible\n      name. Selecting the option saves the request immediately without a separate\n      Save action, closes the picker, and displays the username in the reviewer area;\n      it remains after reload. Each requested reviewer has a button named Remove followed\n      by a space and the username; clicking it immediately removes the request and\n      the username from that area, without a confirmation step. The seed PR is Open,\n      belongs to the signed-in author, and initially has no request or submitted review\n      from the eligible target reviewer; the target is a distinct non-author collaborator\n      with Write or higher permission. Removing a request persists across reload.\n      A “reviewer request” is a pending-review relationship between a PR and a candidate\n      reviewer account, displayed in the Reviewers area on the right side of the PR\n      detail page; it is not the same as a submitted review decision. The author of\n      an Open or Draft PR, Maintain, Admin, or organization Owner may create or delete\n      this relationship. Matching reviewer options update as the user types in the\n      search textbox; pressing Enter or activating a separate search button is not\n      required before selecting an option. A candidate reviewer must have Write, Maintain,\n      or Admin on the repository and must not be the PR author; the system stores\n      or deletes the “PR-reviewer request” relationship together with the operator\n      and time. Removing a request does not delete reviews, comments, or activity\n      records already submitted by that user and does not change the user's effective\n      review decision. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages,\n      or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-6-2-3 (Create a Pull Request\n      from Comparison Results) and REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it\n      must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context\n      changes.\n    scenarios:\n    - name: 'REQ-6-4: Request or Remove Pull Request Reviewers - Scenario 1'\n      steps:\n      - keyword: GIVEN\n        content: 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated\n          browser session. A\n      blocked PR keeps a visible disabled Merge pull request button and explains its\n      unmet review or protection condition before any click. Seed data supplies a\n      Maintain account and separate Open PRs for success and refusal. The eligible\n      PR targets protected main, has a current non-author approval and test: success,\n      and has no conflicts. The blocked PR targets a protected branch and lacks the\n      required valid approval; its disabled button is accompanied by Review required\n      by branch protection. Each seed state is restored independently before reuse. “Merge”\n      is the only write operation that writes the current compare commit''s changes\n      from an Open PR into the base branch; it is not simply marking the PR as complete.\n      Only Maintain, Admin, or organization Owner may merge from the PR detail page\n      using the sole supported “Create a merge commit” method; the merge confirmation\n      area should display satisfied and unsatisfied conditions. Before confirmation,\n      the system rereads the target branch head, current compare commit, merge-conflict\n      state, and protection rules — there must be no valid Request changes; if the\n      target is protected, each enabled rule requirement is enforced independently,\n      requiring 1 valid non-author Approve for the current compare commit when Require\n      1 approval is enabled and requiring `test` success when Require status check\n      test is enabled. An unprotected target has no additional approval-count or check-success\n      requirement, but merging still requires no merge conflicts and no valid Request\n      changes review. After validation succeeds, the system atomically creates a merge\n      commit whose parents are the target-branch head at merge time and the current\n      compare commit, updates the target-branch head, sets the PR to Merged, and stores\n      the merger, time, and resulting commit identifier. If any condition is unsatisfied\n      or persistence fails, the page explains the applicable reason and neither the\n      target branch nor PR changes; a missing required approval displays “Review required\n      by branch protection”. Merged is terminal. This feature builds on the persisted\n      objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-6-3-4\n      (Submit a Pull Request Review) and REQ-6-1 (Protect Branches with Review and\n      Status-Check Requirements); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data\n      consistent after the context changes.'\n    scenarios:\n    - name: 'REQ-6-5: Merge an Eligible Pull Request - Scenario 1'\n      steps:\n      - keyword: GIVEN\n        content: 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated\n          browser session. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`,\n          branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.'\n      - keyword: THEN\n        content: 'After refreshing the PR and `main` branch, the merged status and\n          resulting commit remain; if the PR is a draft, lacks approval, has a failed\n          check, is unmergeable, or the user lacks permission, the merge entry is\n          disabled or rejected and the target branch and PR state remain unchanged.\n          Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches\n          `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.'\n    - name: 'REQ-6-5: Merge an Eligible Pull Request - Scenario 2'\n      steps:\n      - keyword: GIVEN\n        content: 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated\n          browser session. Close pull request immediately sets Closed without\n      an extra confirmation dialog, then Reopen pull request immediately restores\n      Open. After reload, Close pull request is available again. For a viewer who\n      is neither author nor Maintain/Admin/Owner, both controls are absent, rather\n      than merely disabled or rejected after activation. Seed data supplies the author\n      and a separate Read viewer, an authored Open PR for the close/reopen cycle,\n      and a separate Open PR readable by that viewer for permission validation. When\n      the proposed change should not proceed, the PR author, Maintain, Admin, or organization\n      Owner may close an unmerged Open or Draft PR without merging; the same roles\n      may reopen a Closed PR as Open. The system stores the status, operator, and\n      time and records the transition, while keeping the PR discussion, reviews, diff,\n      and branch references viewable; closing or reopening must not update any branch.\n      Merged is terminal, the page does not display close or reopen operations, and\n      the server rejects status changes. This feature builds on the persisted objects,\n      opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-6-2-3 (Create\n      a Pull Request from Comparison Results) and REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing\n      Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after\n      the context changes.\n    scenarios:\n    - name: 'REQ-6-6: Close or Reopen a Pull Request Without Merging - Scenario 1'\n      steps:\n      - keyword: GIVEN\n        content: 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated\n          browser session.
